\omega
be the symplectic form of
\mathcal{P}
.
Based on the Einstein equivalence principle,
we postulate the local existence of
functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu})

and a nondegenerate bivector
\Pi^{\bullet}

on
\mathcal{P}

such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{e^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{mnrs}\hat{y}_{r}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{s}\,=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{m}},{D\hat{y}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\\
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{D\hat{y}^{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{m}p^{n}+p^{m}\hat{y}^{n}}\,\Big)\,,\\[1.79993pt]
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,\delta^{m}{}_{n}\,,\\[2.57996pt]
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,0\,,\\[1.79993pt]
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{m}},{Dp_{n}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,&=\,0\,,\end{split}

(151)

where

e^{m}=e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}
,

D\hat{y}^{m}=d\hat{y}^{m}+\gamma^{m}{}_{n\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\rho}
,
and

Dp_{\mu}=dp_{m}-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{n}{}_{m\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\rho}

utilizes the coframe and spin connection at
x
.
It should be clear that Eq. (
151
)
arises from Eq. (
99
)
by the following replacements
(covariantization map),
which promotes global Lorentz indices to local Lorentz indices:
8
8

8

It should be clear that Eq. (
151
)
can be also stated in terms of

(dx^{\mu},D\hat{y}^{\mu},Dp_{\mu})
:

e^{m}{\>=\>}e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}
,

D\hat{y}^{m}{\>=\>}e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptD\hat{y}^{\mu}
,
and

Dp_{m}{\>=\>}Dp_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptE^{\mu}{}_{m}(x)

are identities.
The sophisticated tetrad language
is employed here
to emphasize the conceptual aspect of the equivalence principle.
Furthermore, Eq. (
151
)
can be also stated in terms of the coordinate differentials

(dx^{\mu},d\hat{y}^{\mu},dp_{\mu})
,
in which case one separates out bare Christoffel symbols on the right-hand sides
(which is against the covariant Poisson bracket philosophy
csg
).

\displaystyle\Pi^{\circ}\,\,\mapsto\,\hskip 1.00006pt\Pi^{\bullet}\,,\quad\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{dx^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptd\hat{y}^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptdp_{\mu}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,\,\mapsto\,\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD\hat{y}^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptDp_{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.

(152)

We refer to Eq. (
151
)
as the
universal covariant Poisson bracket relation

of massive spinning particles,
which reincarnates Eq. (
150
)
in local inertial frames.

Physically,
the universal covariant Poisson bracket relation
Eq. (
151
)
will be regarded as
a consequence of local Poincaré covariance:
diffeomorphism and local Lorentz.

Yet,
any formula deduced from the equivalence principle
could ignore tidal (curvature) effects.
To formulate this idea in a precise fashion,
let
\omega^{\bullet}
be
the pointwise inverse of
\Pi^{\bullet}
,
which defines a nondegenerate two-form on
\mathcal{P}
.
Then the equivalence principle implies that
the particle’s symplectic form
will arise by a curvature correction on
\omega^{\bullet}
:

\displaystyle\omega\,\,=\,\,\omega^{\bullet}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}\qquad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{where}}}}}}\qquad\lim_{R\to 0}\omega^{\prime}\,=\,0\,.

(153)

Surely,

\omega^{\bullet}
and
\omega^{\prime}

must be separately invariant under
coordinate and local Lorentz transformations.
The two-form
\omega^{\prime}
in Eq. (
153
)
is the
covariant symplectic perturbation

for massive spinning particles.

6.3
Model-Agnostic Derivation of QMPD Equations

By combining the ideas in Secs.
6.1
and
6.2
,
we establish
universal recipes
of coupling massive spinning particles to gravity.

Universal Recipe for Minimal Coupling

Again, an important consistency condition
that must be imposed on Eq. (
153
)
is the Liouville property,
i.e.,
the closure
d\omega=0
.
Computation shows that

\omega^{\bullet}
is not closed
iff the spacetime exhibits nonzero curvature.
It can be shown that
the simplest possible covariant symplectic perturbation
that makes
\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
closed is
9
9

9

The proofs of these facts
may check
the Jacobi identities
with the functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu})
,
for
\Pi^{\bullet}
and
\Pi

(cf. covariant Jacobi identity
csg
).
The computations are tedious but doable.
Another method is to utilize symplectic realizations,
which is the exploration in
Sec.
8
in essence.

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,\,=\,\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\,\,=\,\,\frac{1}{2}\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\,\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}\,,

(154)

where
\star
is the Hodge star
acting on local Lorentz indices.

Plugging in
Eq. (
154
) to Eq. (
149
)
yields

\displaystyle{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{e^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\Big(\,{p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip-1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{s}},{{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,

(155)

where
{\alpha}
and
{\beta}
are arbitrary one-forms on the phase space
\mathcal{P}
.

On account of the equivalence principle,
we postulate the local existence of
a vector field
T^{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{P}

such that

\displaystyle\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{e^{m}},\hskip 0.50003pt{T^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,\frac{p^{m}}{m}\,,\quad\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{m}},\hskip 0.50003pt{T^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,0\,,\quad\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{m}},\hskip 0.50003pt{T^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,0\,,

(156)

where
\langle{\,\,\,\,\,},{\,\,\,\,\,}\rangle

denotes the contraction between one-forms and vector fields.
Eq. (
156
) is the covariantization of Eq. (
110
)
via Eq. (
152
).

Let
T
be the time-evolution Hamiltonian vector field
in the interacting theory
such that the Hamiltonian EoM reads
\dot{f}=T[\,{f}\,]
.
Eqs. (
156
) and (
155
) together implies that

\displaystyle\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},\hskip 0.50003pt{T}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},\hskip 0.50003pt{T^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{e^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\Big(\,{p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip-1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,e^{s}{}_{\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,.

(157)

Plugging in the
noncoordinate one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to
{\alpha}
in Eq. (
157
)
readily derives the covariant EoM
in the tetrad frame.
The final result is then
presented in
the coordinate frame as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{x}^{\mu}\,&=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\varepsilon^{\mu\rho\kappa\lambda}\hat{y}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\lambda}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\zeta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip-0.50003pt}\,\Big)\,\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\\
\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,&=\,\frac{1}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\zeta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip-0.50003pt}\,\Big)\,\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\\[1.43994pt]
\frac{Dp_{\mu}}{d\tau}\,&=\,\Big(\,{p_{\zeta}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip-0.50003pt}\,\Big)\,\dot{x}^{\nu}\,.\end{split}

(158)

Eq. (
158
) defines the classical EoM of a consistent Hamiltonian system,
as the modified symplectic form due to Eq. (
154
) is closed.
What is its physical interpretation, then?

MPD Equations

The MPD
Mathisson:1937zz
;
Papapetrou:1951pa
;
Dixon:1970zza
equations
dictate the dynamics of massive spinning particles in curved spacetime
at the leading order in curvature
R

and up to linear coupling in spin
\hat{y}
,
in the regime where derivative effects
are completely suppressed:

R(x)\gg\hat{y}\hskip 1.00006ptDR(x),\hat{y}^{2}\hskip 1.00006ptD^{2}R(x),\cdots
.
In other words,
the MPD equations capture the universal behavior
of massive spinning particles
in the leading pole-dipole, small-curvature approximations.
In this paper,
the MPD equations refer to

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(159a)

\displaystyle\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\hat{p}^{\mu}\hat{p}_{\kappa}\,\Big(\,{-{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(159b)

\displaystyle\frac{Dp^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{-{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(159c)

where the ellipses signify that terms of
\mathcal{O}(R^{1+n})
,
\mathcal{O}(\hat{y}^{1+n})
, or
\mathcal{O}(D^{n}\hskip-1.00006ptR)
are discarded from the right-hand sides
for
n\geq 1
.

The above definition is a refinement of the original result by Ref.
Mathisson:1937zz
;
Papapetrou:1951pa
;
Dixon:1970zza
;
see App.
C.2
for details.
First,
the original references

Mathisson:1937zz
;
Papapetrou:1951pa
;
Dixon:1970zza

utilize the spin tensor
S^{\mu\nu}

instead of the pseudovector

\hat{y}^{\mu}=-{*}S^{\mu\nu}p_{\nu}/p^{2}
.
Second,
the original references

Mathisson:1937zz
;
Papapetrou:1951pa
;
Dixon:1970zza

do not state Eq. (
159a
),
despite its necessity
for well-definedness and consistency
as remarked in Sec.
5.2
.

Straightforward algebra shows that
the EoM obtained in Eq. (
158
)
are equivalent to Eq. (
159
).
Therefore,
in any general-relativistic massive spinning particle model,
the minimal (universal) coupling à la MPD equations
arises if
the covariant symplectic perturbation is given by Eq. (
154
)
in terms of
the physical center position
x^{\mu}

and spin length pseudovector
\hat{y}^{m}

defined in the covariant SSC.

Covariant Double Copy

It is useful to note that
the MPD equations in Eq. (
159
)
are isomorphic to
the
g=0
BMT equations in
Eq. (
114
)
via the replacement

\displaystyle qF_{\mu\nu}(x)\quad\xleftrightarrow{\,\,\,\,\,\,}\quad p_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\lambda}\,=\,-{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 0.50003ptp^{\sigma}\,.

(160)

In our systematic framework,
this “covariant double copy” replacement rule
is nicely explained by
the correspondence between
the (covariant) symplectic perturbations
of electromagnetism and gravity
in Eqs. (
111
) and (
154
)
(to be more precise, see Ref.
csg
).

Universal Recipe for Quadrupolar Coupling

It is possible to incorporate nonminimal couplings in our universal framework.
For instance,
the most general covariant symplectic perturbation
that contributes
to the
\mathcal{O}(R^{1}y^{2})
part of the EoM
for
\hat{y}^{m}
and
p_{m}

is uniquely determined as

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,\,=\,\,\frac{1}{2}\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\,\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 1.00006pte^{r}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{s}\,+\,d\,\bigg(\,{-\frac{C_{2}}{2!}\,p_{m}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{r}e^{s}}\,\bigg)\,,

(161)

where
C_{2}
is a constant.
Eq. (
161
) is bootstrapped by
the physical principles of

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{(a)}\,\,&\text{
Liouville property}\,,\\
\text{(b)}\,\,&\text{
Diffeomorphism invariance}\,,\\
\text{(c)}\,\,&\text{
Local Lorentz invariance}\,,\\
\text{(d)}\,\,&\text{
Parity invariance}\,.\end{split}

(162)

By applying the method demonstrated in Eq. (
157
),
one finds that
the
\mathcal{O}(R^{1}\hat{y}^{1})
contribution to the resulting covariant EoM
arises from the combination

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m}\,\left(\,{\,\begin{aligned} &{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{e^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{s}\\
&+C_{2}\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{D\hat{y}^{r}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{m}{}_{\smash{(nr)s}\vphantom{{\beta}}}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{s}\\
&+\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\alpha}},{Dp_{m}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,R^{m}{}_{\smash{nrs}\vphantom{{\beta}}}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hat{y}^{r}\hskip 0.50003ptp^{s}\end{aligned}}\,\right)\,.

(163)

By inserting
the covariant one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to
{\alpha}
in Eq. (
163
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,\Big(\,{R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)+\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{1-C_{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,=\,-\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{1-C_{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\,\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\frac{Dp^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,=\,-\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(164)

where the ellipses signify that terms of
\mathcal{O}(R^{1+n})
,
\mathcal{O}(\hat{y}^{2+n})
, or
\mathcal{O}(D^{n}\hskip-1.00006ptR)
are discarded from the right-hand sides
for
n\geq 1
.
10
10

10

The last two equations in Eq. (
164
)
are isomorphic to Eqs. (
115b
) and (
115c
)
via
the covariant double copy replacement rule in Eq. (
160
),
upon replacing
c_{1}{\>=\>}g/2
with
C_{2}
.
The first equation in Eq. (
164
)
exhibits a minor mismatch in coefficient
due to the nonabelian nature of gravitational interactions:
the “gravitational charge”
S_{\mu\nu}{\,=\,}\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}
is a variable
instead of a constant
q
.
See App.
C.3
.

QMPD Equations

Eq. (
164
) is a generalization of Eq. (
159
)
by a new parameter
C_{2}

characterizing quadrupolar spin coupling.
In this paper, we refer to Eq. (
164
)
as the QMPD equations.
App.
C

explicates how it agrees
with previous works
Steinhoff:2009tk
;
Harte:2011ku
;
Vines:2016unv
;
Compere:2023alp
;
Ramond:2026fpi
.

Our analysis establishes that
the QMPD equations in Eq. (
164
) provide
the unique and complete
extension of the MPD equations
up to
\mathcal{O}(R^{1}y^{2})
,
when the derivative effects are suppressed.
Our analysis also shows that
the QMPD equations with
the arbitrary quadrupole coefficient
C_{2}

arises in any massive spinning particle model
if the covariant symplectic
perturbation is given as Eq. (
161
),
in terms of
the center position
x^{\mu}

and spin length pseudovector
\hat{y}^{m}

defined in the covariant SSC.

Notably, the effect of a nonzero
C_{2}
on the
\mathcal{O}(y^{2})
EoM
is just as strong as that of the minimal coupling.
The QMPD equations are thus of a phenomenological importance,
as spinning black holes carry a large value
C_{2}{\>=\>}1

Khriplovich:1997ni
;
Thorne:1984mz
;
Hansen:1974zz
;
Newman:1965tw-janis
;
Hernandez:1967zza
;
Thorne:1980ru
.
Any analysis on Kerr black hole orbits
based on the MPD (
C_{2}{\>=\>}0
) equations
is bound to fail,
producing
qualitatively wrong
answers.

6.4
The Four Models with Gravitational Couplings

In Sec.
6.2
,
we have postulated
the universal existence of functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{m},p_{m})

in the general-relativistic phase spaces of massive spinning particles,
satisfying the covariant Poisson bracket relation in Eq. (
151
).
We have also claimed that
the condition in Eq. (
153
) holds
such that the minimal covariant symplectic perturbation is given by Eq. (
154
).
As a result,
Sec.
6.3
has derived
the MPD and QMPD equations
in a universal fashion.

The main mission of
the remaining parts of this paper
is to prove the above postulate and claim
in each of our four concrete models.
The procedure is straightforward for
the spinorial models
because their basic position variables are physical.
The vectorial models,
however,
requires much more work.

We start by providing
the gravitational counterpart of the analysis given in Sec.
5.3
.
The general-relativistic phase spaces will be identified
as per the equivalence principle.
The general-relativistic worldline actions
and their resulting EoM
will be explored.

Spinor Oscillator

The general-relativistic spinor oscillator
is a sigma model
\mathbb{R}{\>\to\>}\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{SO}}_{12}
,
whose target space is

\displaystyle\smash{\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{SO}}_{12}\,\,=\,\,\hskip-1.00006pt\Big(\,{T^{*}\oplus\mathscr{S}^{-}_{\smash{\hskip-0.40974pt\hskip-0.81949pt\times}}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{M}\,.}

(165)

Here,
\mathcal{M}{\>=\>}(\mathbb{R}^{4},g)
is the curved spacetime.
The orthonormal coframe
e^{m}
defines a local trivialization of
T\mathcal{M}
,
which decomposes into the right-handed and left-handed spinor bundles,

\mathscr{S}^{\pm}\hskip-1.00006pt\mathcal{M}
.
Namely,
local Lorentz indices are traded off with spinor indices as

e^{m}\leftrightarrow e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}
.
With this understanding,
the phase space in Eq. (
165
)
is coordinatized by

\displaystyle\smash{\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{x^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.}

(166)

The gravitational minimal coupling
is implemented by the action

\displaystyle\int\,\bigg[\,{\,p_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pte^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\zeta_{\alpha}}{d\tau}-\frac{D\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}{d\tau}\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\,\bigg)-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p^{2}+m^{2}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\,d\tau\,,

(167)

which is a functional of

(x^{\mu},p_{m},\zeta_{\alpha})
and
\kappa^{0}
.
Variations of Eq. (
167
)
derive

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{x}^{\mu}\,&=\,\frac{\kappa^{0}\hskip 0.50003ptp^{\mu}}{m}+\frac{1}{2m^{2}}\,S^{\mu\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\kappa\lambda}R_{\kappa\lambda\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\\
\frac{Dp_{\mu}}{d\tau}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{2}\,S^{\kappa\lambda}R_{\kappa\lambda\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\,,\\
\frac{D\zeta_{\alpha}}{d\tau}\,&=\,\frac{q}{4m^{2}}\,S^{\kappa\lambda}R_{\smash{\kappa\lambda\rho\sigma}\vphantom{{\beta}}}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma^{\rho\nu})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\beta}\,,\end{split}

(168)

and
p^{2}+m^{2}=0
.
Here, we have kept the definitions of

S
,
\xi
,
\hat{y}

in Eqs. (
36
), (
38
), and (
42
)
in local Lorentz frames.
Setting
\kappa^{0}{\>=\>}1
,
Eq. (
168
) is isomorphic to Eq. (
123
)
via covariantization
and the replacement in Eq. (
160
).
Thus it reproduces the MPD equations.

Similarly, the QMPD equations arise by
adding

-(C_{2}/2)\,p_{m}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{m}{}_{nrs}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{r}e^{s}{}_{\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}

in the Lagrangian in Eq. (
167
).
The spin precession is covariantly characterized by

D\zeta_{\alpha}/d\tau=(\Omega_{\smash{\text{QMPD}}})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\beta}
,
where
ambikerr1

\displaystyle(\Omega_{\smash{\text{QMPD}}})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\,=\,-\frac{S^{\kappa\lambda}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\frac{1{\,+\,}C_{2}}{2}\,\bar{R}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}{}_{\kappa\lambda}(x)+\frac{1{\,-\,}C_{2}}{2}\,\hat{p}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,R^{\dot{{\alpha}}}{}_{\smash{{\dot{{\beta}}}\kappa\lambda}\vphantom{{\beta}}}(x)\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(169)

Again,
the isomorphism between Eqs. (
125
) and (
169
) is obvious
and describes Eq. (
160
),
which was observed by Ref.
ambikerr1
.

Massive Twistor

The general-relativistic massive twistor
is a sigma model
\mathbb{R}{\>\to\>}\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}_{16}
,
whose target space is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}_{16}\,\,=\,\,\hskip-1.00006pt\Big(\,{T\oplus\mathscr{S}_{2}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{M}\,.

(170)

By local trivializations,

\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}_{16}

is coordinatized by

\displaystyle\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{x^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006pty^{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.

(171)

The gravitational minimal coupling
is implemented by the action

\displaystyle\int\,\left[\,{\begin{aligned} &{-\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\mu}}+i\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}{d\tau}{\,-\,}\frac{D\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\bigg)\\[-1.20007pt]
&-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{m^{2}-\det(\lambda)\hskip 1.00006pt\det(\bar{\lambda})\hskip-0.50003pt}\,\Big)-\kappa^{1}\,\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\end{aligned}}\,\right]\,d\tau\,,

(172)

which is a functional of

x^{\mu},y^{m},\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}
, and
\kappa^{0},\kappa^{1}
.
Variations of Eq. (
167
)
derive
Eq. (
127
)
with
qF_{\mu\nu}(x)
replaced with

p_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{\lambda}
,
so
it is easy to reproduce the MPD equations.

Similarly, it is easy to derive
the QMPD equations with generic
C_{2}
,
in which case
one finds

D\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip-1.00006pt/d\tau=(\Omega_{\smash{\text{QMPD}}})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}-(i\kappa^{1}/2)\hskip 1.00006pt\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}

with
(\Omega_{\smash{\text{QMPD}}})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}
given in Eq. (
169
).

Vector Oscillator (First Attempt)

The general-relativistic vector oscillator
is the sigma model
\mathbb{R}\to\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}
,
whose target space is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}_{16}\,\,=\,\,\hskip-1.00006pt\Big(\,{T^{*}\oplus T^{\mathbb{C}}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\mathcal{M}\,.

(173)

Via local trivializations,
the phase space in Eq. (
173
)
is coordinatized by

\displaystyle\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.

(174)

Of course, Eqs. (
173
) and (
174
)
covariantize Eqs. (
22
) and (
23
).
Accordingly,
one might naively conclude that
the covariantization of Eq. (
24
)
will define the gravitational minimal coupling.
The first-order action à la Eq. (
121
)
reads

\displaystyle\int\,\left[\,{\,\begin{aligned} &p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\mu}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m}}{d\tau}-\frac{D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{m}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m}}\,\bigg)\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p^{2}+m^{2}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau-\kappa^{1}\hskip 1.00006ptp{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}-\bar{\kappa}^{1}\hskip 1.00006ptp{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{2}-\frac{\bar{\kappa}^{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}^{2}\end{aligned}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\right]\,d\tau\,,

(175)

which is a functional of
({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},p_{m},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m})
and
(\kappa^{0},\kappa^{1},\kappa^{2})
.

Eq. (
175
)
is an action localized on the spurious worldline
\tau\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}(\tau)
in curved spacetime
\mathcal{M}
.
For instance,
local Lorentz transformations act on it
as
p_{m}\mapsto p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt(\Omega^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}))^{n}{}_{m}
,

e^{m}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\mapsto\Omega^{m}{}_{n}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pte^{n}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})
, etc.
The covariant derivatives encode spin connections on the spurious worldline:

D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m}=d\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m}+\gamma^{m}{}_{n\rho}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{n}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho}
.

This could have been a dangerous approach.
First of all, the gravitational fields

e^{m}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})
and
\gamma^{m}{}_{\nu\rho}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})

are coupled to the unphysical center

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}
.
Moreover,
the precise relation between the fake center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}
and the physical center
x^{\mu}

is even lost (yet to be identified) in the general-relativistic case.
Concretely, what is the explicit formula
that expresses
x^{\mu}

as a composite variable
in the defining worldline field basis

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},p_{m},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{m})
?

Fortunately,
the vector oscillator model
might allow for using the action in Eq. (
175
)
since it
mandates the explicit imposition of

p{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}{\>=\>}0=p{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}

by Lagrange multipliers,
on the support of which
one might argue that

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
still coincides with the physical center
x
.

Indeed,
the saddle of Eq. (
175
)
is given in the form of
Eq. (
130
)
via replacing

qF_{\rho\sigma}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})

with

i\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{m}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{m}{}_{n\rho\sigma}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{n}
.
Amusingly,
this replacement rule applies to
the solution to the Lagrange multipliers in
Eq. (
131
) as well.
Consequently,
one finds that
Eq. (
175
)
correctly derives
the MPD equations.
Similarly,
the QMPD equations
can be derived by
adding the term

-(C_{2}/2)\,p_{m}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{m}{}_{nrs}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{n}\hat{y}^{r}\hskip 1.00006pte^{s}{}_{\sigma}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\sigma}

to the Lagrangian in Eq. (
175
).

Note also that a proper refinement of Eq. (
175
)
will be facilitated later by Eq. (
281
).

Vector Oscillator (Second Attempt)

The astute reader will point out that
Eq. (
175
)
describes the analog of the first simplified formulation in Eq. (
129
).
The correct way to couple the vector oscillator to the gravitational background
is to parallel
the second simplified formulation in Eq. (
135
),
in fact.

First,
switch to the alternative coordinate system in Eq. (
132
)
already
at the free theory level.
Second, covariantize
the symplectic potential in Eq. (
134
).
Third, consider integrating out the
scalars

(\kappa^{1},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1})
.
The resulting action is

\displaystyle\int\,\left[\,{\,\begin{aligned} &p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\mu}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hskip-0.50003pt{\alpha}^{m}}{d\tau}-\frac{D\hskip-0.50003pt\bar{\alpha}_{m}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{m}}\,\bigg)\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p^{2}+m^{2}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{2}-\frac{\bar{\kappa}^{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\alpha}^{2}\end{aligned}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\right]\,d\tau\,,

(176)

which is a functional of
(x^{\mu},p_{m},{\alpha}^{m})
and
(\kappa^{0},\kappa^{2})

and is localized on
the physical worldline

\tau\mapsto x^{\mu}(\tau)
.
Eq. (
176
) is a sensible action
for the minimally coupled general-relativistic vector oscillator.

Spherical Top

The general-relativistic spherical top is the sigma model
\mathbb{R}\to\mathcal{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
,
whose target space is

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{20}\,\,=\,\,\hskip-1.00006ptT^{*}\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{F_{\mathrm{\mathrm{SO}^{+}\hskip-0.39864pt(1,3)}}(T\hskip-0.50003pt\mathcal{M})}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.

(177)

Here,

F_{\mathrm{\mathrm{SO}^{+}\hskip-0.39864pt(1,3)}}(T\hskip-0.50003pt\mathcal{M})

is the restricted orthonormal frame bundle
for the pseudo-Riemannian four-manifold

\mathcal{M}{\>=\>}(\mathbb{R}^{4},g)
.
Via local trivializations,
this phase space
is coordinatized by

\displaystyle\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},\hskip 1.00006ptp_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A},\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{mn}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,.

(178)

To implement the gravitational minimal coupling
in the spherical top model,
typical references simply
suppose the covariantized action

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{Wrong:\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{Wrong:}}}\quad\int\,\left[\,{\,\begin{aligned} &p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{n}{}_{B}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta^{AB}\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p^{2}+m^{2}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau-\kappa^{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{a}\end{aligned}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\right]\,d\tau\,,

(179)

which is a functional of

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A},p_{m},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{mn})
and
(\kappa^{0},\kappa^{a})
.

Eq. (
179
)
is a worldline action localized on

\tau\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}(\tau)
.
For instance,
the covariant derivative is

D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A}=d\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A}+\gamma^{m}{}_{n\rho}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{n}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho}
.

The theory which Eq. (
179
) defines
is
seriously ill

due to the spin gauge dependence of the spurious center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}
,
on which the gravitational fields

e^{m}{}_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})
and
\gamma^{m}{}_{n\rho}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})
are coupled to:
the value of the action in Eq. (
179
)
changes when
one changes the SSC from one to another!
The gravitational fields must couple to a physical center
x^{\mu}
,
while
the curved-spacetime generalization
of the formula in Eq. (
52
)
is missing.
However, we do not have the definition of the
physical center
x^{\mu}

in terms of
the defining variables

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{m}{}_{A},p_{m},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{mn})
,
such that
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=0
.
In fact, we do not even know
if such a gauge-invariant center exists at all!

Due to the utterly nonsensical status of Eq. (
179
),
we shall avoid paying attention to its EoM.
The correct answer will be
given in Eq. (
216
).
We have checked that
Eq. (
216
)
consistently derives
the MPD and QMPD equations
in
any
SSC
just like in Sec.
5.3
.

7
The Interacting Theory of Spherical Top

In Sec.
6.4
,
we have observed that
the interacting theory of the spherical top
involves subtleties
regarding
the spin gauge invariance
of the background-coupled action.

To reiterate,
the interacting theory develops fatal inconsistencies
if the action is
coupled to the spin gauge-dependent center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.
Namely,
one’s customary choice of the SSC

changes the predictions on physical observables
.
It is a strict necessity
to realize the
external field couplings
at a spin gauge-invariant center
x
.

It is the very mission of this section to establish
the interacting theory of the spherical top model
in a rigorous fashion
and prove the existence of a spin gauge-invariant center
x
.
This mandates a nontrivial analysis,
which must explicitly identify

(a)

the spin gauge-invariant center coordinates and actions,

(b)

their explicit formulae
in the defining worldline field basis,

(c)

the spin gauge algebra and flow in the presence of interactions,

(d)

accurate realizations of the universal covariant structures postulated in Sec.
6.2
.

To reiterate,
the most pressing problem is that
the explicit formula for the physical center in Eq. (
52
)
is lost in curved spacetimes,
since
it is nonsensical to add
the
coordinates

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}

with (the components of) a
vector

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}p_{\nu}/p^{2}
.
To this end, we again utilize the strategy taken in Sec.
6.1
:
“flat spacetime in noninertial frames”
as a bridge toward general relativity.

7.1
Explicit Parametrization of Spin Gauge Redundancy

Our point of departure
is a deeper understanding on the spin gauge transformations
at the level of free theory.

Spin Gauge-Invariant Observables

The free spherical top describes a
20
-dimensional phase space
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{20}

whose coordinates are
({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A},p_{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu})
,
equipped with the three spin gauge generators
\phi_{a}
in Eq. (
53
)
that define the symplectic quotient

\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{14}
in Eq. (
54
).

A function
f
on
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{20}

is
spin gauge invariant

iff it descends to the quotient
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{14}
:

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,0\,.

(180)

For example,
the universal variables
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu})

are spin gauge invariant:

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptp_{\nu}\,,\quad\hat{y}^{\mu}\,=\,-\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,{*}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptp_{\nu}\,,\quad p_{\mu}\,.

(181a)

The
14
-dimensional quotient
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{14}

can be explicitly coordinatized by finding
14
spin gauge-invariant variables
that are functionally independent.
As noted in Sec.
2.2
,

(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu})
together describes an
11
-dimensional space.
It is known
Steinhoff:2015ksa

that
the remaining three spin gauge-invariant variables are

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\,:=\,\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}-\frac{\hskip 0.50003pt{(\hat{p}{\,+}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0})^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt}}{\hat{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}(\hat{p}{\,+}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0})}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}\,.

(181b)

Direct computation shows that

\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}},{\phi_{c}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=0

and
p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}=0
.
In sum,
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu},\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a})

in Eq. (
181
)
provides a well-behaved,
manifestly spin gauge-invariant
coordinate chart on
the physical phase space

\mathds{P}_{14}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
.

Geometrically, the formula in Eq. (
181b
)
originates from the minimal Lorentz boost
that aligns the spurious body frame
with the momentum

Steinhoff:2015ksa
:

\displaystyle\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}-\frac{\hskip 0.50003pt{(\hat{p}{\,+}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0})^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt(\hat{p}{\,+}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0})_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt}}{\hat{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}(\hat{p}{\,+}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{0})}-2\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{\mu}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{\nu 0}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\begin{pmatrix}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{0}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}\hat{p}^{\mu}\\
\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(182)

We will refer to
\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}
—more accurately the Lorentz matrix
\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{A}=(\hat{p}^{\mu},\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a})
—as the
physical body frame
.
The physicalness of
\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}

is expected from
the perspective of
Eq. (
71
).

Decomposition into Physical and Gauge Parts

Given the clear identifications of the
14
physical variables

(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu},\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a})

characterizing the physical phase space
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{14}
,
we now investigate
how the unphysical redundancies are wired in
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}_{20}
.

First of all, let us define

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\,:=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptp_{\nu}\,,\quad S_{\mu\nu}\,:=\,\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}\,,

(183)

so
p{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}=0
and
S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptp^{\nu}=0
.
The interpretation of
S_{\mu\nu}
is the physical spin tensor.
The vector
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
describes the
separation between the physical and spurious centers:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}\,=\,x^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt+\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\,.

(184)

It can be shown that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip-1.00006pt\,=\,S_{\mu\nu}-({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}p)_{\mu\nu}\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\,=\,\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{B}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{B}{}_{A}\,,

(185a)

where
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{A}{}_{B}
is a Lorentz group element defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{A}{}_{B}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt})\,&=\,\frac{1}{1{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt/4}\,\Bigg(\,{\,\hskip-0.50003pt{\begin{matrix}{1{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt/4}&-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}_{b}\\
-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}&({1{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}^{2}\hskip-1.00006pt/4})\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{a}{}_{b}+{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}_{b}/2\end{matrix}}\,}\,\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(185b)

Here,
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
is a three-vector
that exhibits a nonlinear relationship with
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\hskip-0.24994pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt})\,=\,\Lambda^{\mu a}\,\frac{1}{|p|}\bigg[\,{\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{S_{\rho\sigma}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\rho}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\sigma}{}_{b}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{b}}{2}-\bigg(\,{1-\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}^{2}}{4}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{a}}\,\bigg]\,.

(185c)

In this way,
the spurious variables

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu})

are found as functions of the physical variables

(x^{\mu},\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{A},S_{\mu\nu})

and a three-vector
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
.
That is, we have shown that
a single three-vector variable
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
\bm{\vec{\beta}}

parametrizes the entire gauge content of the spurious variables.

Further computation

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\nu}{}_{B}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{A}{}_{C}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{B}{}_{D}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta^{CD}\,=\,\Big(\,{\hat{p}^{[\mu}+{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{[\mu}{}_{0}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu]}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\theta^{\circ}\,&=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu A}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd\Lambda^{\nu a}+\phi_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,.\end{split}

),

\displaystyle\exp\hskip-1.00006pt\Big(\,{\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{\phi_{c}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\hskip 1.00006pt\sigma^{c}}\,\Big)\,\,\,:\,\,\,\,\left\{\hskip 1.00006pt{\begin{array}[]{rl}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}&\,\,\,\mapsto\,\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt})+{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{\sigma})\\

where we have made indices implicit
to avoid clutter.
The three-vector variable

The submanifold
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}=0

.

It should be clear that
the gauge orbit in Eq. (

)
solves

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}}{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}&\,,\quad\frac{\partial\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}}{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,,\\
\frac{\partial p_{\mu}}{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{\mu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}&\,,\quad\frac{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}}{\partial{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.\end{split}

(196)

)

-dimensional submanifold

in

and

.

7.2
The Physical Center in General Relativity

Our next mission is to explicitly construct the physical center
x^{\mu}

in the general-relativistic theory of spherical top.

Flat Spacetime Revisited

We begin with revisiting the free spherical top
in curvilinear coordinates.
Eq. (
184
) states that
the fake and physical centers
are related by
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}=x^{\mu}+{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
.
Crucially,
this equation can only make sense in
Cartesian coordinates

in which the concept of “position vector” is valid.

What is the coordinate-free content of Eq. (
184
)?
Geometrically, it states that the spurious center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
is reached from the physical center
x
by moving along the straight-line path in the direction of the vector
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
.
A well-known fact is that straight lines are the geodesics of flat space.
Therefore, Eq. (
184
) states that the spurious center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
is obtained by the
geodesic flow
from the physical center
x

generated by the vector
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
.

Based on this understanding,
Eq. (
184
) appears in curvilinear coordinates as

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\,x^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt+\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,}\,\bigg)\,,

(197)

where
\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)
are the Christoffel symbols
of the flat metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}(x)
.
Eq. (
197
) is easily extended to all orders in
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
by solving the geodesic equation
with the initial condition
(x^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu})
.
A pedagogical exercise is to deduce Eq. (
197
)
by directly performing a Cartesian-to-curvilinear coordinate transformation on Eq. (
184
).

In Eq. (
197
),
we have started employing the Synge bitensor notation
Ruse:1931ht
;
Synge:1931zz
;
Poisson:2011nh
;
gde
.
The idea of this notation is to employ
separate index sets—primed and unprimed—when studying
bilocal objects

in differential geometry.
As illustrated in Fig.
4
,
the central object of investigation
in our case
is a spacelike geodesic segment,
whose endpoints provide two spacetime points
x
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.
Unprimed indices
\mu,\nu,\rho,\sigma,\cdots
are assigned to the physical point
x
.
Primed indices
\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\cdots
are assigned to the spurious point
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.
Tensors based at
x
carry unprimed indices.
Tensors based at
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
carry primed indices.
The former transform with the Jacobian factors at
x
.
The latter transform with the Jacobian factors at
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.

In this fashion, one practices the very philosophy of index notation:
the transformation behavior of an object
is completely specified by—and is evident from—its index form.

As shown in Fig.
4
,

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
is the tangent vector of the geodesic segment
at the point
x
;
hence it carries an unprimed index.
Namely,
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}

is an element of
the tangent space at
x
.

Figure 4:

A geodesic connects between
the physical center
x^{\mu}
and the fake center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
,
along which the parallel propagator
W^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}
is constructed
so that local tensor degrees of freedom
are transported like

p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}=p_{\mu^{\prime}}
.

Parallel Propagator

To compare between
tensors at
x
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
,
a law of parallel propagation is necessary:
the very mathematical idea of a connection.

The parallel propagator is an object of the index form

W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}
,
which describes an isomorphism between
the tangent spaces at

x
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.
In the Synge framework

Ruse:1931ht
;
Synge:1931zz
;
Poisson:2011nh
;
gde
,
it is a common practice to
consider the parallel propagator
due to the Levi-Civita connection
about the geodesic contour.
The explicit power series expansion for

W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}

in this case reads

\displaystyle\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}-\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{\hskip 0.24994pt\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho,\sigma}(x)-2\hskip 1.00006pt\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\lambda\rho}(x)\,\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\nu\sigma}(x)\hskip-0.50003pt}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})}\,\bigg)\,.

(198)

It a nice exercise to derive
Eq. (
198
)
from
a path-ordered exponential
constructed on the geodesic contour
from
x
to
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.

The parallel propagator facilitates
transporting tensors at point
x
to point
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}

(and vice versa).
For example, a vector
v^{\mu}
at
x
is parallel-transported to
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
as
v^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}=W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptv^{\mu}
.
Similarly, a covector
p_{\mu}
at
x
is parallel-transported to
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
as

p_{\mu^{\prime}}=p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}
,
as is visualized in Fig.
4
.
In general,
a rank-
(p,q)
tensor at
x
is parallel-transported to
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
as

\displaystyle T^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{1}\cdots\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{p}}{}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{1}\cdots\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{q}}\,=\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{1}}{}_{\mu_{1}}{\cdots\hskip 1.00006pt}W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}_{p}}{}_{\mu_{p}}\,T^{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}}{}_{\nu_{1}\cdots\nu_{q}}\,W^{\nu_{1}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}_{1}}{\cdots\hskip 1.00006pt}W^{\nu_{q}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}_{q}}\,,

(199)

where our notation is such that

\displaystyle W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\kappa}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\,=\,\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\,,\quad W^{\mu}{}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}\,=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,.

(200)

In fact, this gymnastics can be applied to tensor-valued differential forms as well.
Especially, it is left as an exercise to check the following identities
regarding vector-valued differential one-forms,
in flat spacetime:

\displaystyle D^{2}=\hskip 1.00006pt0

\displaystyle{\quad\implies\quad}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{dx^{\mu}+D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}}\,\Big)\,,

(201a)

\displaystyle D^{2}=\hskip 1.00006pt0

\displaystyle{\quad\implies\quad}Dv^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptDv^{\mu}\,.

(201b)

The second identity assumes the relation

v^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}=W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptv^{\mu}
.
Also,

D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}=d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}+\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\rho}
,

Dv^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}=dv^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\Gamma^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006ptv^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
,
and

Dv^{\mu}=dv^{\mu}+\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptv^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\rho}
.
Our index notation
is supposed to unambiguously clarify
the position which the connection coefficients inside the covariant derivatives
couple to.

Symplectic Structure Revisited

Finally,
let us apply the Synge formalism to the free spherical top
to rederive Eq. (
188
)
in curvilinear coordinates.
The first line now reads

\displaystyle\theta^{\circ}\,

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}\,.

(202)

To proceed,
we not only use Eq. (
197
)
but also
stipulate that the local tensor degrees of freedom
of the spherical top
are parallel-transported
to the physical center
as

\displaystyle p_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\,=\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\nu}{}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,.

(203)

By plugging in Eqs. (
197
) and (
203
)
to Eq. (
202
),
we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}\theta^{\circ}\,&=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu A}\,,\end{split}

(204)

through using the identities in Eq. (
201
).
Since Eq. (
204
) involves only first-order differentials,
the computation in Eq. (
187
)
readily generalizes to yield

\displaystyle\theta^{\circ}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a}+\phi_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,,

(205)

provided the definitions in
Eqs. (
183
) and (
185
)
are kept the same.

7.3
The Spin Gauge-Invariant Action

We may now transition to curved spacetime

\mathcal{M}=(\mathbb{R}^{4},g)
.
Suppose there exists a physical center
in the presence of curvature.
Then its relation with the fake center
must smoothly approach Eq. (
197
)
in the flat limit.
With hindsight,
let us simply postulate that
the relation in Eq. (
197
)
does not develop curvature corrections.

Similarly,
we would like to
again utilize the geodesic parallel propagator due to the Levi-Civita connection
of the curved metric
g
,
to transport local tensor degrees of freedom.
Importantly, this implies
that our parallel propagator
will be metric-preserving:

\displaystyle g_{\mu\nu}(x)\,W^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\nu}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\hskip-1.00006pt\,=\,g_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,,\quad g_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptW^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}\,=\,g_{\mu\nu}(x)\,.

(206)

Minimal Coupling Done Right

Next, we recall from Sec.
7.2

that
two equivalent formulations of the free spherical top
are provided by
Eqs. (
202
) and (
205
)
in the limit of vanishing curvature.
Let us reproduce them for convenience:

\displaystyle\theta^{\circ}\,

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}\,,

(207a)

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a}+\phi_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,.

(207b)

Both of Eqs. (
207a
) and (
207b
)
are generally covariant.
Thus,
following the strategy that has been repeatedly practiced in this paper,
we might transplant
either of the symplectic potentials in
Eqs. (
207a
) and (
207b
)
to curved spacetime.

However,
the astute reader will point out that
one of Eqs. (
207a
) and (
207b
)
is not sensible
in view of spin gauge invariance.
Moreover,
the mathematical equivalence between Eqs. (
207a
) and (
207b
)
breaks down
in the presence of spacetime curvature,
as the identities in Eq. (
201
)
gain curvature corrections.

Based on our preliminary explorations in Sec.
6.4
,
the answer should be obvious:
the correct symplectic potential is Eq. (
207b
).
Thus, we declare that
the symplectic potential
of the general-relativistic spherical top
in curved spacetime
is

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a}+\phi_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,.

(208)

Here,
it is important that

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}
describes a set of
scalars
,
carrying no spacetime indices,
so
d\beta^{a}=D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}
:
the index
a\in\{1,2,3\}
is a global index,
not gauged.

Figure 5:

Flipside view on Fig.
4
.
The physical center
x^{\mu}

is reached from the fake center
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

through deviating by the tangent vector
-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
.

Action In the Original Worldline Field Basis

To return to the primed variables

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}},p_{\mu^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}})
,
we reverse the derivation in Eqs. (
202
), (
204
), and (
205
)
while taking curvature effects into account.
We find that

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}+{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}+{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}\,,

(209)

where
\vartheta^{\prime}_{(0)}
and
\vartheta^{\prime}_{(1)}

are curvature corrections
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to 0}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}\,=\,0\,,\quad\lim_{R\to 0}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}\,=\,0\,,\quad\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbf{x}}\to x}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}\,=\,0\,,\quad\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbf{x}}\to x}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}\,=\,0\,.

(210)

Let us elaborate on the details in four steps.

First,
we retain the definitions in
Eqs. (
183
) and (
185
)
to bring Eq. (
208
) to

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu A}\,,

(211)

which merges the physical and gauge parts
for the rotational sector.

Second, Eq. (
197
) states that

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
is obtained by the unit-time geodesic flow from
x

by
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
.
Its inverse relation reads

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\,=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\bigg(\,{\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,}\,\bigg)\,,

(212)

meaning that
the physical center
x^{\mu}

is obtained by
the unit-time geodesic flow
from
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

by
-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
.
Here,

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}=W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}

is the tangent vector of the geodesic segment
at
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}

that points outwards,
so
-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

is the tangent vector pointing inwards.
As mentioned before,
we stipulate that
the local tensor variables in Eq. (
211
),

p_{\mu}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}
,
are parallel-transported
to the spurious point
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}

along the geodesic path
to become

p_{\mu^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}
,
respectively.

Third,
we generalize
the identities in Eq. (
201
)
in the presence of curvature.
Due to general covariances at both
x
and
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
,
they will be generalized in the following form:

\displaystyle W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,,

(213a)

\displaystyle W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptDv^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,Dv^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt+\Big(\,{\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,v^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,.

(213b)

Here,

X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}
,
and

\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}

are all tensorial at
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
.
Therefore,
they must arise by combining
the Riemann tensor
R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})
,
their covariant derivatives,
and the vector
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
.
In particular,

X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

and

Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

are known as the
Jacobi propagators

Dixon:1970zza
;
Dixon:1974xoz
,
whose all-orders formulae
were studied by Ref.
Vines:2014oba
.
Meanwhile, the recent work
gde

has provided
a versatile and powerful formalism
that systematically generates
all-order formulae
for

\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}
,
and

\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}
.

Finally,
using the identities in Eq. (
213
)
brings Eq. (
211
)
into the form in Eq. (
209
),
with
the curvature corrections being

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}\,

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,}\,\Big)\,,

(214a)

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,}\,\Big)\,.

(214b)

Here, we have defined

\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}:=X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}

and

\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}:=Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}-\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
.
It should be clear that
the relations in Eqs. (
183
) and (
185
)
imply
