\displaystyle-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}p_{\nu^{\prime}}\,,\quad S_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\hskip-1.00006pt\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}+({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}p)_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\,=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\delta}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\,.

(215)

In conclusion,
the spin gauge-invariant action
of the general-relativistic spherical top model
is explicitly found as

\displaystyle\int\,\left[\,{\,\begin{aligned} &p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}}{d\tau}\\
&+p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\sigma^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\xi{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}p_{\xi^{\prime}}}{p^{2}}}\,\bigg)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{\delta}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\bigg(\,{\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\sigma^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\xi{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}p_{\xi^{\prime}}}{p^{2}}}\,\bigg)}\,\bigg)\\
&-\frac{\kappa^{0}}{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{g^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.24994ptp_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+m^{2}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau-\frac{\kappa^{a}}{2}\,\Big(\,{\hat{p}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{0}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\end{aligned}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\right]\,d\tau\,,

(216)

which is a functional of

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A},p_{\mu^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}})
and
(\kappa^{0},\kappa^{a})
.
The all-orders formulae due to Ref.
gde

state that

\displaystyle\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{(-1)^{2}}{2}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{(-1)^{3}}{6}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{4})\,,

(217a)

\displaystyle\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{(-1)^{2}}{6}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{(-1)^{3}}{12}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{4})\,,

(217b)

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{(-1)^{1}}{1}R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{(-1)^{2}}{2}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,,

(217c)

\displaystyle\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{(-1)^{1}}{2}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{(-1)^{2}}{3}\,R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,,

(217d)

and there is no difficulty in
proceeding to higher orders
or even
writing down
a closed-form expression (finite sum) at each order in
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
.

We should highlight the fact that
nonlinear-in-Riemann terms start to appear
from the first omitted spin orders in Eq. (
217
).
For instance,
the
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{4})
part of
\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
is

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{4}}{4!}\,\Big(\,{R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime};\lambda^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})+R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\xi^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\xi{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\kappa^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})}\,\Big)\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,.

(218a)

The
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{6})
part of
\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
is, schematically,

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{6}}{6!}\,\Big(\,{{(D^{4}\hskip-1.00006ptR)}+6\,{(D^{2}\hskip-1.00006ptR)}\hskip 1.00006ptR+4\,{(D\hskip-0.50003ptR)}\hskip 0.50003pt{(D\hskip-0.50003ptR)}+R\hskip 1.00006pt{(D^{4}\hskip-1.00006ptR)}+R\hskip 1.00006ptR\hskip 1.00006ptR}\,\Big)\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}\,.

(218b)

Note that the power of Riemann tensors
is unbounded.

Crucially,
the constraints do not change their forms
in Eq. (
216
)
because
they are
scalars
and
the parallel propagator is metric-preserving.
This demonstrates the importance of Ref.
Kim:2021rda
’s refinement
on the spin gauge generators
in the gravity-coupled theory.
The metric-preserving property also ensures that
the geodesic flow from
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}
to
x

commutes with
merging the physical and gauge parts
in the above derivation.
Lastly,
it should be also clear that our methodology here
readily applies to nonminimal couplings.

Fictitious Interaction Lagrangian

Clearly,
working in noncovariant gauges
costs us infinitely many extra interaction vertices;
their implications for
post-Newtonian expansions
will be commented in Sec.
9
.
We may want to describe such extra terms
with the adjective “fictitious,”
as their physical meaning is reminiscent of the
fictitious forces:

\displaystyle\theta\,\,=\,\,p\hskip 1.00006ptdx\,+\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,S\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda\,+\,\phi_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\,\,=\,\,p\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}\,+\,\overbrace{{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}+\mathrlap{\vphantom{\Big|}}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}}^{\hbox{\raise 2.0pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{
\footnotesize Fictitious
\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{
\footnotesize Fictitious
}}}}}}}}}}\,.

(219)

The perspectives toward gauge redundancies
may be twofold.
On the one hand,
gauge redundancies are a useful utility
that allows one to
describe the same physics
in different “reference frames.”
A typical example is
a free particle in special relativity
described in curvilinear coordinates:
changing coordinates
generates fictitious forces
in the equations of motion,
but the physical observables are the same.
This perspective
observes the beauty of gauge “symmetries”
and their constraining power in writing down interaction Lagrangians.

On the other hand,
gauge redundancies are
an absurdity in our description,
sometimes serving as sheer sources of confusion.
A typical argument
appeals to the Stueckelberg trick

stuckelberg1938wechselwirkungskrafte
,
which explicates that
gauge redundancies can be arbitrarily introduced or not.
This perspective emphasizes the fakeness of gauge redundancies
and their null physical content.

In our case,
the spin gauge “symmetry”
has granted us the power of
changing the SSC freely
by the Hamiltonian flows of
\phi_{a}
.
Once the
time evolution is
known in one SSC,
one readily determines
the time evolution in other SSCs by spin gauge transformations.

At the same time,
the spurious description
on the fake worldline
\tau\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}(\tau)

of one’s choice
has generated
an infinite tower of fictitious terms
in the interaction Lagrangian,
which are needed to
keep the observables on the physical worldline the same.
11
11

11

In fact, the transition from Eq. (
208
)
to Eq. (
209
),
summarized in Eq. (
219
),
may be thought of as a Stueckelberg trick
that introduces
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

as a reference point.

For instance,
suppose one arbitrarily picks a worldline

\tau\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}(\tau)

that exhibits a certain wiggly behavior.
To generate the newly introduced
accelerations of this worldline
and
precessions of the accordingly parallel-transported local tensor variables,
the force law—EoM—must change.
This is precisely the meaning and role
of the fictitious terms
found in Eq. (
216
):
the fictitious forces
due to changing the SSC (as a “frame”) from covariant to noncovariant.

7.4
Preservation of Spin Gauge Flow and Algebra

Lastly,
we provide some vital consistency checks
on our constructions.

Spin Gauge Flow with Electromagnetic Couplings

We begin with the electromagnetic coupling.
To this end, we apply our geometric framework in Sec.
5
to the spherical top model.
Let
\omega^{\circ}

be the free-theory symplectic form
given to
\mathds{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
,
so
d\omega^{\circ}=0
.
Suppose
a symplectic perturbation

\omega^{\prime}
on
\mathds{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}

such that

d(\omega^{\circ}+\omega^{\prime})=0
.

The spin gauge transformations
are generated by
the Hamiltonian vector fields

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\circ}\,:=\,{\Pi^{\circ}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\quad\mathscr{G}_{a}\,:=\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}

(220)

in the free and interacting theories,
respectively.
Are these vector fields the same?

We identify a sufficient condition for
a spin gauge-invariant symplectic perturbation:

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt0{\quad\implies\quad}\pounds_{\mathscr{G}^{\circ}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}\,=\,d\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\mathscr{G}^{\circ}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}+\iota_{\mathscr{G}^{\circ}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006ptd\omega^{\prime}\,=\,0\,.

(221)

Provided Eq. (
221
),
we recall Eq. (
109
)
to find

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{a}\,\,=\,\,\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\circ}\,-\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\iota_{\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\circ}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}},{{\,\,\,\,\,}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\,=\,\,\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\circ}\,.

(222)

Therefore, the spin gauge flow
is completely ignorant of the symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}
.
In the Poisson bracket notation, Eq. (
222
)
boils down to

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,,

(223)

for any function
f
on
\mathds{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
.
Thus the gauge orbit
in the interacting theory
is identical
to the
\mathbb{R}^{3}
found in Eq. (
7.1
),
and the abelian gauge algebra is left intact:

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,0\,.

(224)

All of the electromagnetic couplings considered in this paper satisfy the sufficient condition in Eq. (
221
).
For instance, the minimal coupling is given by

\omega^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\,F_{\mu\nu}(x)\,dx^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\nu}
.
Since
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\nu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}=0
,
this implies

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,\,=\,\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}-\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{x^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,F_{\mu\nu}(x)\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\nu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,\,=\,\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,.

(225)

Spin Gauge Flow with Gravitational Couplings

Next, we consider the gravitational coupling.
To this end, we apply our geometric framework in Sec.
6
to the spherical top model.
By the postulate in Sec.
6.2
,
the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}

is equipped by
a nondegenerate two-form

\omega^{\bullet}
.
As will be shown shortly in Sec.
8.4
,
the explicit construction of
\omega^{\bullet}

shows that

d\omega^{\bullet}=-d(p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu})
,
so the claim in Sec.
6.3
is verified.
Suppose a covariant symplectic perturbation

\omega^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}

such that

d(\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime})=0
.

Let us define

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\bullet}\,:=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\quad\mathscr{G}_{a}\,:=\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}

(226)

in the free and interacting theories,
respectively.
Our postulates imply that

\lim_{R\to 0}\mathscr{G}_{a}=\mathscr{G}^{\bullet}_{a}
.
Are these vector fields the same?

We identify a sufficient condition for
a spin gauge-invariant covariant symplectic perturbation:

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\bullet}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}\hskip 1.00006pt=\hskip 1.00006pt0{\quad\implies\quad}\pounds_{\mathscr{G}^{\bullet}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}\,=\,d\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{\mathscr{G}^{\bullet}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}+\iota_{\mathscr{G}^{\bullet}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006ptd\omega^{\prime}\,=\,-\iota_{\mathscr{G}^{\bullet}_{a}}\hskip 1.00006ptd\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,0\,.

(227)

In Eq. (
227
),
the last equality follows from
the condition

d\omega^{\bullet}=-d(p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu})

and

\displaystyle{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{\mu}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{\mu}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,0\,.

(228)

Eq. (
228
)
arises by covariantizing the Poisson brackets in the free theory:
see Eq. (
289
),
for instance.
Provided Eq. (
227
),
we recall Eq. (
149
)
to find

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}_{a}\,\,=\,\,\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\bullet}\,-\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{\iota_{\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\bullet}}\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\prime}},{{\,\,\,\,\,}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\,=\,\,\mathscr{G}_{a}^{\bullet}\,.

(229)

Therefore, the spin gauge flow
is completely ignorant of the covariant symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}
.
To clarify,
this means that tidal effects can be ignored
when computing Poisson brackets with
the gauge generators
\phi_{a}
.
Clearly, the abelian gauge algebra is left intact:

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\bullet}\,=\,0\,.

(230)

Again, the last equality in Eq. (
230
)
follows by covariantization; see Sec.
8.4
.

Crucially, the definitions and identities used in the above derivation
count heavily on the fact that

d\phi_{a}=D\phi_{a}
.
It cannot be overemphasized that
it is significantly important to
formulate the spin gauge generators
as spacetime scalars.

All of the gravitational couplings considered in this paper satisfy the sufficient condition in Eq. (
227
).
For instance, the minimal coupling descirbes

\omega^{\prime}=p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}
.
Via Eq. (
228
),
this implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,\,&=\,\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{df},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{df},{dx^{\rho}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\Big(\,{p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}}\,\Big)\,\hskip 0.50003pt{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\sigma}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\\
\,\,&=\,\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{df},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,.\end{split}

(231)

In contrast,
the Poisson algebra of
spin gauge-invariant observables
is clearly sensitive to the covariant symplectic perturbation.

Finally, the
\mathbb{R}^{3}
gauge orbit can be explicitly identified.
The action of

\exp\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{\phi_{c}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\hskip 1.00006pt\sigma^{c}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)
on
\mathcal{P}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}_{20}

is found as

\displaystyle\left\{\hskip 1.00006pt{\begin{array}[]{rl}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}&\,\,\,\mapsto\,\,\,\mathrm{Exp}\Big(\,{{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{\sigma})},\hskip 1.00006pt{\mathrm{Exp}\Big(\,{{-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt})},\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}}\,\Big)}}\,\Big)\\
p_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}&\,\,\,\mapsto\,\,\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}&\,\,\,\mapsto\,\,\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{B}\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{-1}\hskip-0.50003pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{R}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{\sigma})}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\hskip-0.50003pt{}^{B}{}_{A}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}&\,\,\,\mapsto\,\,\,\begin{aligned} &{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,W^{\nu}{}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\\
&\vphantom{\frac{0}{0}}+\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}){\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,W^{\nu}{}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{\sigma}){\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\end{aligned}\end{array}}\,\hskip 1.00006pt\right\}\,,

where the unprimed, single-primed, and double-primed
indices are associated with
the gauge representatives at

0
,

\bm{\vec{\beta}}
\bm{\vec{\beta}}
,
and

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{\vec{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.50003pt}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}\vec{\sigma}
,
respectively.
In particular,

W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}

describes the geodesic contour
from the physical center to the original fake center,
while

W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}

describes the geodesic contour
from the physical center to the transformed fake center.
Note that

W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptW^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\neq W^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}

in the presence of spacetime curvature.
The definitions in Eq. (
185
)
are kept at each point,
while
\mathrm{Exp}

denotes the exponential map:
geodesic deviation by

v{\>\in\>}T_{x}\mathcal{M}

from
x{\>\in\>}\mathcal{M}

yields the point
\mathrm{Exp}(v,x){\>\in\>}\mathcal{M}
.
To put it simply,
Eq. (
7.4
) reincarnates Eq. (
7.1
)
in the Riemann normal coordinate system
based at the physical center.
Eq. (
7.4
) implements
the spin gauge transformations
by always referencing
the physical center
x

as the point of a special status,
consistently with the fact that the gauge algebra is abelian.

7.5
Bottom-Up Approach to Spin Gauge-Invariant Coupling

In the above analysis,
we have provided a top-down construction
of the spin gauge-invariant gravitational coupling.
This top-down approach
first insists on the existence of a physical center
(Sec.
7.2
)
and
by construction
makes it physical afterwards
(Sec.
7.4
).
It identifies a working proposal
for the spin gauge-invariant coupling,
from which
the action in the original field basis is completely determined as well
(Sec.
7.3
).

The reader could question
whether every aspect of this procedure was inevitable.
For instance,
one might ask if
the formulae in Eqs. (
197
) and (
198
)
can be generalized to
more complicated forms
that possibly involve the local tensor degrees of freedom,
although
there exist arguments why the geodesic deviation in Eq. (
197
)
and the parallel propagation with respect to the Levi-Civita connection in Eq. (
198
)
are natural options in Einstein gravity where metric dictates the entire geometry.

To present a complementary perspective,
this subsection provides a bottom-up construction of the spin gauge-invariant gravitational coupling.
We demonstrate that the spin gauge-invariant action,
in the original field basis,
can be perturbatively constructed
by imposing the preservation of the gauge algebra and flow
as bootstrap conditions.
We sketch this analysis
at a few low orders in
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
,
providing a brief justification for our top-down results.

Electromagnetism

The top-down derivation of
the spin gauge-invariant
electromagnetic interaction symplectic potential
has been given in Eq. (
136
):

qA_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}
with
x^{\mu}={\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}
.
It can be shown
gde
that

qA_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}
is equivalent to

qA_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}+{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{charge}}

modulo total derivative,
where the fictitious symplectic potential is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{charge}}\,=\,q\,\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\left(\,{\begin{aligned} &F_{\rho_{1}\mu,\rho_{2}\cdots\rho_{\ell}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{2}}{\cdots}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{\ell}}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}\\
&+(\ell{\,-\,}1)\,F_{\rho_{1}\mu,\rho_{2}\cdots\rho_{\ell-1}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{2}}{\cdots}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{\ell-1}}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\end{aligned}}\,\right)\end{split}

(236)

so that
\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.40974pt\mathbf{x}}\to x}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{charge}}=0
.
Eq. (
236
) can be viewed as providing the corrections to the spurious minimal coupling
qA_{\mu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}

that restore spin gauge invariance
at every order in
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
.
The interaction symplectic form is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{charge}}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\,qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt})\hskip 1.00006ptd({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt})^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt})^{\nu}\,,

(237)

where the right-hand side is readily Taylor-expanded in the orders of
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
.

Our message here is that the fictitious symplectic form in Eq. (
237
)
can be discovered
from scratch
in the following fashion.
The point of departure is the free spherical top described in Sec.
3.3
.
Its symplectic form is

\displaystyle\omega^{\circ}\,

\displaystyle=\,dp_{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu A})+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu A}\,,

(238)

whose pointwise inverse
derives the Poisson brackets in Eq. (
50
).
It follows that

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,w^{\mu}{}_{a}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}=-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{w_{a}\wedge p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}^{\mu\nu}\,,

(239)

where we have introduced a notation

\displaystyle w^{\mu}{}_{a}\,:=\,\frac{1}{2|p|}\,\hat{\eta}^{\mu\nu}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\nu\rho}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho}{}_{a}\,.

(240)

A naive attempt to endow a charge
supposes the spurious minimal coupling

\omega=\omega^{\circ}+\frac{1}{2}\,q\hskip 0.50003ptF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}
.
By utilizing a geometric series expansion,
one finds that
the resulting Poisson bracket describes

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,&=\,\begin{aligned} &\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}-\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\hskip 1.00006ptqF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\\
&+\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\hskip 1.00006ptqF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\hskip 1.00006ptqF_{\rho\sigma}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}-\cdots\,,\end{aligned}\\
\,&=\,qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,w^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu}{}_{b}\,,\end{split}

(241)

which is nonvanishing.
Apparently, the gauge algebra is deformed
and is not closed.

To fix this problem, the most general modification of the symplectic form is

\displaystyle\omega\,=\,\omega^{\circ}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\,qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\qquad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{where}}}}}}\qquad d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,=\,0\quad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{and}}}}}}\quad\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.1024pt}\to 0}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,=\,0\,.

(242)

Due to the condition

\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.1024pt}\to 0}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}=0
,
the viable ansatz seems to be

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\frac{1}{2}\,f_{\mu\nu}^{(\ell,0)}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}+f_{\mu\nu}^{(\ell,1)}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\,f_{\mu\nu}^{(\ell,2)}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu}}\,\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(243)

where
f_{\mu\nu}^{(\ell,j)}

is of
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\ell-j})
for
\ell{\>\geq\>}j

and
f_{\mu\nu}^{(\ell,j)}=0
for
\ell{\><\>}j
.
It suffices to set
f_{\mu\nu}^{(0,0)}=0
.

With this ansatz,
we examine the implication of the condition

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(244)

At the leading order in
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}
,
using Eq. (
239
) gives

\displaystyle 0\,

\displaystyle=\,\Big(\,{qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})-2\hskip 1.00006ptf^{(1,1)}_{[\mu\nu]}+f^{(2,2)}_{\mu\nu}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu}{}_{b}\,,

(245)

so
f^{(1,1)}_{\mu\nu}=f^{(2,2)}_{\mu\nu}=qF_{\mu\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})

qualifies as a solution.
Proceeding to higher orders, it can be checked that
the following qualifies as a solution:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt{}&{}-\frac{1}{2}\,qF_{\mu\nu,\rho}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}-qF_{\rho\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\\
{}&{}+q\,\sum_{\ell=2}^{\infty}\,\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{\ell!}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{3}}{\cdots}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{\ell}}\hskip 1.00006pt\left(\,{\begin{aligned} &\tfrac{1}{2}\,F_{\mu\nu,\rho_{1}\cdots\rho_{\ell}}\hskip-0.50003pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{2}}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\\
&+F_{\rho_{1}\mu,\rho_{2}\cdots\rho_{\ell}}\hskip-1.00006pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{1}}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{2}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\,F_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2},\rho_{3}\cdots\rho_{\ell}}\hskip-1.00006pt({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{1}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho_{2}}\end{aligned}}\,\right)\,.\end{split}

(246)

Certainly, Eq. (
246
)
reproduces
the interaction symplectic form described in Eq. (
237
).

Gravity

For gravity,
the point of departure
of the bottom-up construction
is the covariantization of Eq. (
238
):

\displaystyle\omega^{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}\,

\displaystyle=\,Dp_{\mu^{\prime}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A})+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}\,.

(247)

The resulting covariant Poisson brackets
simply covariantize those in Eq. (
50
).
That is,
the nonvanishing components of
the pointwise inverse
\Pi^{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}

of Eq. (
247
)
in the generally covariant (yet not spin gauge-invariant) basis

(d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}},Dp_{\mu^{\prime}},D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A},D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\hskip-0.50003pt)

of one-forms
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{Dp_{\nu^{\prime}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\,&=\,\,\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}}\,,\\
{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}},{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\,&=\,\,\Big(\,{-2\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{[\rho^{\prime}}\eta_{\sigma^{\prime}]\nu^{\prime}}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\,,\\
{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}},{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\,&=\,\,\Big(\,{-4\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{[\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{[\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\eta_{\nu^{\prime}][\rho^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{\lambda^{\prime}]}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}]}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\,.\end{split}

(248)

It follows that

\displaystyle{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\,=\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\quad{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{w_{a}\wedge p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,,

(249)

where

\displaystyle w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\,:=\,\frac{1}{2|p|}\,\hat{\eta}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\,.

(250)

It is easily seen that Eq. (
247
) is not symplectic:

d\omega^{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}\neq 0
.
The minimal correction is

\omega=\omega^{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}-\frac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.2048pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
.
By utilizing a geometric series expansion,
one finds that
the resulting Poisson bracket describes

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\\
&\,=\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.2048pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})}\,\Big)\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}+\cdots\,,\\
&\,=\,\Big(\,{-\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.2048pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})}\,\Big)\,w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,,\end{split}

(251)

which is nonvanishing.
Apparently, the gauge algebra is deformed
and is not closed.

To fix this problem, the most general ansatz is

\displaystyle\omega\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\omega^{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\tfrac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.2048pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,,

(252a)

where

\displaystyle d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,=\,0\qquad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{and}}}}}}\qquad\lim_{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.1024pt}\to 0}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,=\,0\,.

(252b)

To automate the closure
d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}=0
,
we can take
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}=d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}
.
Let
\Pi
be the pointwise inverse of
\omega
in Eq. (
252a
).
We stipulate the abelian spin gauge algebra
as a bootstrap condition:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,.

(253)

The astute reader will point out that
there exists a rough isomorphism between the current problem
and the former problem,
by recalling Eq. (
160
).
Namely, the “covariant double copy” correspondence
identifying spin as a gravitational charge
(local Lorentz charge)
arises between Eqs. (
242
) and (
252a
),
yet in terms of spurious variables:

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\,qF_{\mu\nu}(x)\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\quad\xleftrightarrow{\,\,\,\,\,\,}\quad-\tfrac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.2048pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,.

(254)

Therefore,
an educated guess for the fictitious symplectic potential is a sum of

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s1)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,&=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,,\\
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s2)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,&=\,\hskip 1.00006pt-\tfrac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\cdots\,,\end{split}

(255)

and so on.
This directly applies the replacement rule in Eq. (
254
)
to the electromagnetic fictitious symplectic potential in Eq. (
236
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{charge}}\,=\,-qF_{\rho\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{qF_{\rho\nu}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu}+\cdots}\,\Big)+\cdots\,.\end{split}

(256)

For simplicity of our demonstration,
we ignore terms involving derivatives of external fields.

By construction, the exterior derivative of Eq. (
255
)
is given by
the sum of
(a)
a group of terms isomorphic to the electromagnetic fictitious symplectic form in Eq. (
246
)
via Eq. (
254
)
and
(b)
a group of new contributions
due to the non-constancy of the gravitational charge
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}
:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s1)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,

\displaystyle=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\tfrac{1}{2}\,D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\wedge R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\cdots

(257)

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s2)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\,

\displaystyle=\,\hskip 1.00006pt-\tfrac{1}{4}\,D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\wedge R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\cdots\,.

These new terms are essentially corrections due to symplecticity
and the nonabelian nature of the Lorentz group.

The perturbation on the Poisson bracket
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}

due to
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s1)}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{-\tfrac{1}{2}}\,R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{w_{a}\wedge p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\,w^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}_{b}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}w^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,,\end{split}

(258a)

where the last equality uses the algebraic Bianchi identity.
Similarly,
the perturbation on the Poisson bracket
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}

due to
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s2)}}

is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tfrac{1}{4}\,R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{4}\,R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{w_{a}\wedge p}\hskip 1.00006pt\big){}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}_{b}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}w^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,,\end{split}

(258b)

where the last equality also arises via the algebraic Bianchi identity.
As a result, the calculations in Eqs. (
258a
) and (
258b
)
are identical except the coefficients.
Their sum is

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}w^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,.

(259)

To cancel out Eq. (
259
),
we may consider a class of terms that do not directly reference the spurious spin.
A generic ansatz reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m2)}}\,&=\,\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\,\Big)\,,\\
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m3)}}\,&=\,\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m3)}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\,\Big)+\cdots\,.\end{split}

(260)

These extra fictitious terms
probe the effects of the other gravitational charge,
i.e., mass
(diffeomorphism charge).
Again, we wish to ignore higher-order terms
involving derivatives of the Riemann tensor.

The perturbation on the Poisson bracket
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}

due to
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m2)}}=d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m2)}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,\Big(\,{2\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{(\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu^{\prime})\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\,\Big)\,\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d\phi_{a}},{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}\Big(\,{R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})+R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda^{\prime}}w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}\Big(\,{R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})+\tfrac{1}{2}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda^{\prime}}w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}-(a{\,\leftrightarrow\,}b)\\
&\,=\,3\hskip 1.00006pt\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda^{\prime}}w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,,\end{split}

(261a)

which uses the algebraic Bianchi identity.
Similarly,
the perturbation on the Poisson bracket
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}

due to
{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m3)}}=d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m3)}}
is

\displaystyle 3\hskip 1.00006pt\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m3)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda^{\prime}}w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,,

(261b)

where we have ignored the derivative term.
Recall from Eq. (
249
) that

{\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}={\Pi^{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bullet}}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}
.
The sum of Eqs. (
259
), (
261a
), and (
261b
) gives

\displaystyle\Big(\,{\tfrac{1}{2}+3\hskip 1.00006pt\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}+3\hskip 1.00006pt\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m3)}}}\,\Big)\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda^{\prime}}w^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{b}\,.

(262)

Thus, additional information is needed to fix the unknown coefficients

\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}
and
\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m3)}}
.

To this end, we stipulate the spin gauge invaraince of the momentum-squared
as another bootstrap condition:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\tfrac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{2}},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,p^{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{\mu^{\prime}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,.

(263)

We have considered four types of fictitious terms so far:

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s1)}}
,

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s2)}}
,

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m2)}}
,
and

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m3)}}
.
Among these,
only

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(s1)}}

and

{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{(m2)}}

contribute to the spin gauge flow of
p_{\mu}
.
Computation shows that
the sum of
their contributions to the Poisson bracket

{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{\mu^{\prime}}},{d\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}
is
