\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,w^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}-p_{\kappa^{\prime}}w^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+2\hskip 1.00006pt\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}w^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{(\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime})\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

(264)

Again, the first term in Eq. (
264
),
which directly references the spurious spin
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}
,
arises by a calculation isomorphic to the electromagnetic case.
The other terms
are specific to gravity.
Contracting Eq. (
264
) with
p^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,p^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003ptw^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}-p_{\kappa^{\prime}}w^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,p^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\\
&-\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}w^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,p^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}\,p^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptw^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,p^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,,\end{split}

(265)

where we have used the index symmetries
of the Riemann tensor.
The role of first term in Eq. (
265
)
is to cancel the contribution from the
spurious minimal coupling.
The other terms in Eq. (
265
)
cancel each other iff

\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m2)}}{\>=\>}{-1/2}
,
in which case
Eq. (
262
) implies

\mathtt{k}_{\text{(m3)}}{\>=\>}{1/3}
.
This completely fixes the unknown coefficients.

In summary, we have established a bottom-up determination
of the fictitious symplectic potential
in the regime where derivatives of the Riemann tensor are ignored,
by imposing the preservation of the spin gauge algebra,
Eq. (
253
),
and the spin gauge invariance of the momentum-squared,
Eq. (
263
):
12
12

12

In principle, one can also examine the spin gauge invariance of the physical spin-squared.

\displaystyle\begin{split}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,\,=\,\,&\tfrac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-\tfrac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\\
&-\tfrac{1}{2}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\tfrac{1}{3}\,p_{\kappa^{\prime}}R^{\kappa{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\lambda{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\cdots\,.\end{split}

(266)

Eq. (
266
) shows perfect match with the top-down answer
presented in Eq. (
214
):

1/2-1=-1/2

and

-1/6+1/2=1/3
.
The correspondence in Eq. (
254
)
determines the fictitious terms due to the local Lorentz charge (spin),
which,
through symplecticity constraint
d{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\varpi^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}=0
and the nonabelian nature of the Lorentz group,
induces the fictitious terms due to the diffeomorphism charge (mass).

8
Covariant Geometry of General-Relativistic Phase Spaces

Eventually,
we can explicitly prove
the postulates and claims
of Secs.
6.2
and
6.3

for each of the four models.
The structure of our exposition below
shall follow Ref.
csg
.

8.1
Spinor Oscillator

The action in Eq. (
167
) describes the symplectic potential

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,p_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pte^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}D\zeta_{\alpha}-D\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\,\Big)\,.

(267)

The exterior derivative of Eq. (
267
)
splits as

\omega=d\theta=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
First,

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,Dp_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}-i\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006ptD\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D\zeta_{\alpha}+D\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\,D\zeta_{\alpha}-D\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\,\Big)

(268)

covariantizes the free theory’s symplectic form.
Second, the curvature correction
\omega^{\prime}
arises by the square
D^{2}
of the covariant exterior derivative:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime}\,&=\,\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}D^{2}\zeta_{\alpha}-D^{2}\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\,\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\zeta_{\beta}-\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\dot{{\beta}}}{}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\zeta_{\alpha}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\xi}^{\alpha}\bar{R}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\alpha}-\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\dot{{\alpha}}}{}_{\dot{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\xi^{\dot{{\beta}}}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{mn}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{mn}\,=\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{n}\,.\end{split}

(269)

Any term involving no
D^{2}

is reproduced by the covariantization
\omega^{\bullet}
.

A complete noncoordinate basis of one-forms
and its dual
are given
on
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{SO}}_{12}
as

\displaystyle\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptDp_{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD\zeta_{\alpha},\hskip 1.00006ptD\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\quad\xleftrightarrow{\,\,\,\text{dual}\,\,\,\hskip 0.81949pt}\quad\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{\alpha}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(270)

which transform covariantly.
Here,
\tilde{E}_{m}
is the horizontal lift

ehresmann1948connexions
;
Mason:2013sva
;
gde

of the spacetime tetrad
E_{m}=E^{\mu}{}_{m}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\partial_{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{r}\,=\,E^{\rho}{}_{r}(x)\,\bigg(\,{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\rho}}+p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\gamma^{n}{}_{m\rho}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}-\gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}{}_{\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\beta}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{\alpha}}-\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{\dot{{\beta}}}{}_{{\dot{{\alpha}}}\rho}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(271)

As a result, the pointwise inverse of Eq. (
268
)
is explicitly found as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi^{\bullet}\,\,=\,\,\begin{aligned} &\tilde{E}_{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{\alpha}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}+\frac{1}{-2p^{2}}\,S^{mn}\hskip 1.00006pt\tilde{E}_{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\tilde{E}_{n}\\
&+\frac{(\sigma^{mn}\zeta)_{\alpha}}{2p^{2}}\,p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\tilde{E}_{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{\alpha}}+\frac{(\bar{\zeta}\hskip 0.24994pt{\bar{\sigma}}^{mn})_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}{2p^{2}}\,p_{n}\hskip 1.00006pt\tilde{E}_{m}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,.\end{aligned}\end{split}

(272)

The universal covariant Poisson bracket relation is verified.
Plugging in the one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to Eq. (
272
)
reproduces Eq. (
151
).

The minimal covariant symplectic perturbation in Eq. (
154
)
is verified.
Eq. (
269
) is the simplest curvature correction such that

d\omega{\>=\>}dd\theta{\>=\>}0
by construction.

Consequently,
we establish the existence of
the universal functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{m},p_{m})

in the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}_{12}^{\mathrm{SO}}
of the spinor oscillator.

8.2
Massive Twistor

The action in
Eq. (
172
)
describes the symplectic potential

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,-\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}+i\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003ptD\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}{\,-\,}D\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\Big)\,.

(273)

The exterior derivative of Eq. (
273
) is

\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
First,
\omega^{\bullet}

covariantizes the free theory’s symplectic form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\begin{aligned} &\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}i\hskip 0.50003ptDy^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\wedge D\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}-D\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\wedge\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}i\hskip 0.50003ptDy^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\hskip 1.00006pt\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}-2i\hskip 1.00006ptD\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\wedge y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}D\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,.\end{aligned}\kern-5.0pt\end{split}

(274)

Second,
the curvature correction
\omega^{\prime}

arises by
D^{2}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime}\,&=\,i\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003ptD^{2}\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}{\,-\,}D^{2}\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,i\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 0.50003pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\beta}}}}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\dot{{\beta}}}{}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\,-\,}\bar{R}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 0.50003pt\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,-i\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{{\alpha}{\dot{{\beta}}}}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\dot{{\beta}}}{}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}+i\hskip 1.00006ptp_{{\beta}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 1.00006pty^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}\bar{R}_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\,=\,p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pt{\star}R^{m}{}_{n}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{n}\,.\end{split}

(275)

Any term involving no
D^{2}

is reproduced by the covariantization
\omega^{\bullet}
.

A complete noncoordinate basis of one-forms and its dual are given
on
\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}
as

\displaystyle\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{e^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptDy^{m},\hskip 1.00006ptD\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I},\hskip 1.00006ptD\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\quad\xleftrightarrow{\,\,\,\text{dual}\,\,\,\hskip 0.81949pt}\quad\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{m},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{m}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}}\,\bigg)\,,

(276)

which transform covariantly.
The horizontal lift is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{r}\,=\,E^{\rho}{}_{r}(x)\,\bigg(\,{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\rho}}-\gamma^{m}{}_{n\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pty^{n}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{m}}-\gamma_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}{}_{\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}-\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\beta}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\bar{\gamma}^{\dot{{\beta}}}{}_{{\dot{{\alpha}}}\rho}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}}\,\bigg)\,.

(277)

As a result,
the pointwise inverse of Eq. (
274
)
is explicitly found as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Pi^{\bullet}\,\,=\,\,\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}\,(\bar{\lambda}^{-1})^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}I}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 1.00006pt\wedge}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}}\,\bigg)+\frac{1}{2}\,(\lambda^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\lambda}_{I{\dot{{\alpha}}}}}\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 1.00006pt\wedge}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}-i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}}\,\bigg)\\
&-\frac{i}{2}\,y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\beta}}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1}\lambda^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\alpha}}\,\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}-i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 1.00006pt\wedge}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\tilde{E}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}+i\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{{\dot{{\alpha}}}{\alpha}}}}\,\bigg)\,.\end{aligned}\end{split}

(278)

The universal covariant Poisson bracket relation is verified.
Plugging in the one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to Eq. (
278
)
reproduces Eq. (
151
).

The minimal covariant symplectic perturbation in Eq. (
154
)
is verified.
Eq. (
275
) is the simplest curvature correction such that

d\omega{\>=\>}dd\theta{\>=\>}0
by construction.

Consequently,
we establish the existence of
the universal functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{m},p_{m})

in the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}
of the massive twistor.

8.3
Vector Oscillator

Physical Coupling

To identify the physical coupling
for the vector oscillator,
we gather the preliminary explorations in
Eqs. (
175
) and (
176
)
and the technique demonstrated with the spherical top in Sec.
7
.

At the free theory level,
we have reformulated
the symplectic potential in Eq. (
24
)
into the form of Eq. (
134
)
by splitting out a complex gauge component
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}
.
We reproduce this reformulation
in the setup of
flat spacetime in noninertial frames:

\displaystyle\theta^{\circ}\,

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,,

(279a)

\displaystyle=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\alpha}^{\mu}-D\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{\mu}}\,\Big)+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}-d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}}\,\Big)\,.

(279b)

Just like in Eq. (
207
),
both are healthy in terms of general covariance
but one is spoiled due to spin gauge noninvariance.
Of course,
the correct answer is Eq. (
279b
)
as discussed in Eq. (
176
).
The gravitational fields are coupled at the physical center,
x^{\mu}
.

We transplant Eq. (
279b
)
to curved spacetime.
By recalling Eq. (
133
),
we then impose

{\alpha}^{\mu}=\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\nu}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}=-i\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}
,
and
Eq. (
197
)
with

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu}\,=\,-\frac{1}{|p|}\,\Big(\,{{\alpha}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}+\bar{\alpha}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}}\,\Big)\,.

(280)

We also stipulate that the local tensor degrees of freedom
such as
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}
,
{\alpha}^{\mu}
,
p_{\mu}

are transported by the geodesic parallel propagator.
The result is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\theta\,&=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptD{\alpha}^{\mu}-D\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{\mu}}\,\Big)+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}-d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}}\,\Big)\\[3.60004pt]
\,&=\,\begin{aligned} &p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptD{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}\\
&+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{\mu^{\prime}}D\hskip-0.50003pt{\alpha}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-D\hskip-0.50003pt\bar{\alpha}_{\mu^{\prime}}{\alpha}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\Big)+{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}-d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}}\,\Big)\,,\end{aligned}\\[3.24005pt]
\,&=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptd{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,+\,\underbrace{{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{mass}}+\mathrlap{\vphantom{\Big|}}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{\vartheta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.12497pt}\kern-4.0pt}^{\vphantom{\prime}}_{\text{spin}}}_{\hbox{\raise-2.0pt\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{
\footnotesize Fictitious
\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{
\footnotesize Fictitious
}}}}}}}}}}\,.\end{split}

(281)

Again, it is important that
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}
is spacetime scalar.

In conclusion,
the symplectic potential
of the general-relativistic vector oscillator,
minimally coupled at the physical center,
develops fictitious terms
in the defining chart
of
\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}

in Eq. (
174
).
They precisely take the identical form as in
Eq. (
214
)
arising from the tensors

\mathit{\Delta}X^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

\mathit{\Delta}Y^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
,

\smash{\acute{X}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}
,
and

\smash{\acute{Y}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}

listed in Eq. (
217
),
yet with the definition

\displaystyle-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,\hskip 1.00006pti\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}-{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}}\,\Big)\,p_{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,.

(282)

Symplectic Form

We shall now compute the symplectic form.
Taking exterior derivative on the unprimed expression in Eq. (
281
)
gives

\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
First,

\displaystyle\omega^{\bullet}\,

\displaystyle=\,Dp_{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\mu}+i\,D\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}D{\alpha}^{\mu}+i\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\psi}}}}_{1}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\psi}}}_{1}

(283)

covariantizes
the alternative representation of
the free theory’s symplectic form.
Second, the curvature correction
\omega^{\prime}
arises by the square
D^{2}
of the covariant exterior derivative:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime}\,&=\,\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptD^{2}{\alpha}^{\mu}-D^{2}\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{\mu}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{i}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{\nu}+\bar{\alpha}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\nu}{}_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}^{\mu}}\,\Big)\,=\,i\,\bar{\alpha}_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\alpha}^{\nu}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 0.24994pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\,.\end{split}

(284)

In the last line,
we have imported
the definition of
\hat{y}^{\mu}
in Eq. (
27
)
within
T_{x}\mathcal{M}
.

Poisson Structure

Eq. (
283
) defines a nondegnerate two-form on
\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{MT}}}
,
so its pointwise inverse
\Pi^{\bullet}
is well-defined.
Based on the isomorphism
between Eq. (
283
)
and the exterior derivative of Eq. (
134
)
(covariantization map with respect to the physical center),
it is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{dx^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,S^{\mu\nu}&\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{Dp_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,,\\
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{D{\alpha}^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,{\alpha}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}&\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D{\alpha}^{\mu}},{D\bar{\alpha}_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-i\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,,\end{split}

(285)

where
S^{\mu\nu}=i\hskip 1.00006pt({\alpha}^{\mu}\bar{\alpha}^{\nu}-\bar{\alpha}^{\mu}{\alpha}^{\nu})
.

Universality

We establish the existence of
the universal functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{m},p_{m})

in the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}_{16}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}
of the vector oscillator.
Explicitly, define

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{\mu}\,&=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\bigg(\,{\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,}\,\bigg)\,,\\
\hat{y}^{\mu}\,&=\,\frac{i}{-p^{2}}\,\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\lambda}+\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\lambda\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{2})}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{\lambda}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\varepsilon^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}p_{\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}_{\rho^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\sigma^{\prime}}\,,\\[1.43994pt]
p_{\mu}\,&=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.50003pt\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\delta^{\lambda}{}_{\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\mu\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{2})}\,\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(286)

and
\hat{y}^{m}=e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\mu}
,

p_{m}=p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptE^{\mu}{}_{m}(x)
.

The universal covariant Poisson bracket relation is verified.
Plugging in the one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to
\Pi^{\bullet}
described in Eq. (
285
)
reproduces Eq. (
151
).

The minimal covariant symplectic perturbation in Eq. (
154
)
is verified.
Eq. (
284
) is the simplest curvature correction such that

d\omega{\>=\>}dd\theta{\>=\>}0
by construction.

Note that it was necessary to go through the whirlwind trip
from Eqs. (
279
) to (
281
).
If one naively takes Eq. (
279b
)
as the symplectic potential
in curved spacetime,
one finds

Dp_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\wedge d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+i\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\wedge D\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{i}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}

for the symplectic form,
which is wrong:
the apparent covariant symplectic potential is given at the fake center,
failing
the postulate in Eq. (
154
).

The refinement of Eq. (
175
)
arises by taking the action as

\int\theta-(\kappa^{0}\hskip-1.00006pt/2m)\hskip 1.00006pt(\hskip 0.24994pt{p^{2}+m^{2}}\hskip 0.50003pt)\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau-\kappa^{1}\hskip 1.00006ptp{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}-\bar{\kappa}^{1}\hskip 1.00006ptp{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\hskip-0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}-{\kappa^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{2}/2-{\bar{\kappa}^{2}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}^{2}/2
,
where
\theta
is the pullback of Eq. (
281
).

8.4
Spherical Top

Symplectic Form

The action in
Eq. (
216
)
uses the symplectic potential in Eq. (
209
),
which is equivalent to Eq. (
208
).
The exterior derivative of Eq. (
208
) is

\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}
.
First,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\bullet}\,=\,Dp_{\mu}\wedge dx^{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\,DS_{\mu\nu}\wedge(\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a})+\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptD\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\wedge D\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a}+d\phi_{a}\wedge d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\pdfliteral\hbox to0.0pt{$\bm{{\beta}}$\hss}\pdfliteral\mbox{$\bm{{\beta}}$}}\hskip 0.24994pt}^{a}\end{split}

(287)

covariantizes the free theory’s symplectic form
in Eq. (
189
).
Second,
the curvature correction
\omega^{\prime}

arises by the square
D^{2}
of the covariant exterior derivative:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega^{\prime}\,&=\,\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptD^{2}\hskip-0.24994pt\Lambda^{\nu a}}\,\Big)\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt\Lambda^{\rho a}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&=\,\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{\nu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\eta}^{\rho\mu}}\,\Big)\,=\,-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\mu\nu}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 0.24994pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\,.\end{split}

(288)

In the last line,
we have put

\hat{y}^{\mu}=-{*}S^{\mu\nu}p_{\nu}/p^{2}

within
T_{x}\mathcal{M}

by recalling Eq. (
52
).

Again, it is important to use the correct symplectic potential in Eq. (
207b
).
If one mistakenly uses Eq. (
207a
)
in curved spacetime,
the symplectic form is found as

Dp_{\mu^{\prime}}\wedge d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\frac{1}{2}\,D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}\wedge({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptD\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A})+\frac{1}{2}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}D{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A}\wedge D\hskip-1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}A}-\frac{1}{4}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}}R^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}\hskip-0.40974pt}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\,d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}d{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}
,
which does not include any fictitious terms.
It is left as an exercise to derive the fictitious terms for the symplectic form
by either
applying the methods of Ref.
gde

to Eqs. (
287
) and (
288
)
or taking exterior derivative on Eq. (
209
).

Poisson Structure

Eq. (
287
) defines a nondegnerate two-form on
\mathcal{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
,
so its pointwise inverse
\Pi^{\bullet}
is well-defined.
Again, based on the isomorphism
between Eq. (
287
)
and the free-theory symplectic form in Eq. (
189
)
(covariantization map with respect to the physical center),
it is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{dx^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,S^{\mu\nu}&\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{DS_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,2\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\mu}{}_{\smash{[\rho}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\sigma]}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\,,\\
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{Dp_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}&\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{DS_{\mu\nu}},{DS_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\Big(\,{-4\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{[\kappa}{}_{[\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\eta}_{\nu][\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\lambda]}{}_{\sigma]}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptS_{\kappa\lambda}\,,\\
{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}},{dx^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt\Lambda^{\nu}{}_{a}&\,,\quad{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{a}},{DS_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,\Big(\,{-2\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{[\rho}\hat{\eta}_{\sigma]\nu}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\nu}{}_{a}\,.\end{split}

(289)

Universality

We establish the existence of
the universal functions
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{m},p_{m})

in the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}_{20}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
of the spherical top.
Their explicit formulae are

\displaystyle\begin{split}x^{\mu}\,&=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\bigg(\,{\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.24994pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\,\Gamma^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}})\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{3})\,}\,\bigg)\,,\\
\hat{y}^{\mu}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-2p^{2}}\,\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\lambda}+\Gamma^{\mu}{}_{\lambda\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{2})}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{\lambda}{}_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 1.00006pt\varepsilon^{\mu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}p_{\nu^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho^{\prime}\hskip-0.81949pt\sigma^{\prime}}\,,\\[1.43994pt]
p_{\mu}\,&=\,p_{\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 0.50003pt\delta^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{\lambda}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\delta^{\lambda}{}_{\mu}-\Gamma^{\lambda}{}_{\mu\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{\rho}+\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\bm{b}\hskip 0.12497pt}^{2})}\,\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(290)

where
\hat{y}^{m}=e^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\mu}
,

p_{m}=p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptE^{\mu}{}_{m}(x)
.
Just as Eq. (
286
),
Eq. (
290
) is highly nonlinear.

The universal covariant Poisson bracket relation is verified.
Plugging in the one-forms

(e^{m},D\hat{y}^{m},Dp_{m})

to
\Pi^{\bullet}
described in Eq. (
289
)
reproduces Eq. (
151
).

The minimal covariant symplectic perturbation in Eq. (
154
)
is verified.
Eq. (
288
) is the simplest curvature correction such that

d\omega{\>=\>}dd\theta{\>=\>}0
by construction.

9
Further Applications

9.1
Dynamical Symmetries and Conserved Charges

This subsection demonstrates how
model-ignorant conserved charges
are directly uncovered from
the universal Poisson and covariant Poisson bracket relations.
For simplicity, we limit our attention to spacetime isometries.

Symmetry in Hamiltonian Mechanics

Let us first review the definition of symmetry in Hamiltonian mechanics.
Suppose a Hamiltonian system defined by
a symplectic form
\omega
and a Hamiltonian
H

on a phase space.
A vector field
V
implements
an infinitesimal symmetry transformation on
this system iff
both of the following conditions are met:

\displaystyle\text{Symplectic Condition}:

\displaystyle\quad\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega\,=\,0\,,\,\,

(291a)

\displaystyle\text{Hamiltonian Condition}:

\displaystyle\quad\pounds_{V}H\,=\,V[\,{H}\,]\,=\,0\,.\,\,

(291b)

Due to the Cartan formula
and the closure of
\omega
,
the symplectic condition
translates to
d(\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega)=0
,
which implies local existence of a function
Q

such that

\displaystyle\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega\,=\,-dQ{\quad\iff\quad}V\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{Q}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(292)

Here,
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{f}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=\omega^{-1}({\,\,\,\,\,},df)
denotes the Hamiltonian vector field of
f
.
This shows that symmetries in Hamiltonian mechanics
are realized as Hamiltonian actions on the phase space.
In other words, we have approached the Noether theorem
in the symplectic geometry language:
the function
Q

is the Noether charge.

On the other hand, the Hamiltonian condition
encodes compatibility with the system’s
time-evolution generator
(or constraints if any).
Plugging in Eq. (
292
),
one finds that it encodes
Poisson commutativity of
Q
and
H
:

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{H},{Q}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,V[\,{H}\,]\,=\,0\,.

(293)

We shall also note that the above definition
does not invoke the symplectic potential
\theta
.
Famously, the symplectic potential
involves redundancies
\theta\sim\theta+\mathit{\Delta}\theta

for any closed one-form
\mathit{\Delta}\theta
on the phase space.
Thus it cannot provide an invariant definition of symmetry.

Instead, the role of the symplectic potential
in this discussion
would be
a customary gadget
that provides a convenient formula
for the Noether charge.
By using the freedom
\theta\sim\theta+\mathit{\Delta}\theta
,
one may find a particular
\theta
such that

\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\theta=0
.
In this case, the Cartan formula asserts

\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega=-d\iota_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\theta
,
leading to a concrete identification of the Noether charge as

\displaystyle\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\theta\,=\,0{\quad\implies\quad}Q\,=\,\iota_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\theta\,.

(294)

In fact, such a symmetry-preserving symplectic potential

\theta
will give rise to a natural, symmetry-adapted phase space action.

Symmetry-Preserving Symplectic Perturbations

Next, we develop a general theory of symmetries
in the framework of symplectic perturbations.
Suppose
(\mathds{P},\omega^{\circ},H)
defines the free theory of a Hamiltonian system.
Suppose a vector field
V
on
\mathds{P}

implements an infinitesimal symmetry transformation on
(\mathds{P},\omega^{\circ},H)
,
whose Noether charge is
Q^{\circ}
:

\displaystyle\iota_{V}\omega^{\circ}\,=\,-dQ^{\circ}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{H},{Q^{\circ}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,V[\,{H}\,]\,=\,0\,.

(295)

A closed two-form
\omega^{\prime}
on
\mathds{P}

is a
symmetry-preserving symplectic perturbation

iff

\displaystyle\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}\,=\,0\,.

(296)

Again,
through the Cartan formula
and
d\omega^{\prime}{\>=\>}0
,
Eq. (
296
)
translates to
d(\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime})=0
,
which implies local existence of a function
Q^{\prime}

such that

\displaystyle\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}\,=\,-dQ^{\prime}\,.

(297)

Via Eq. (
295
), Eq. (
297
) implies that

\displaystyle\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega\,=\,-dQ\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{H},{Q}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,V[\,{H}\,]\,=\,0

(298)

where
Q=Q^{\circ}+Q^{\prime}
and
\omega=\omega^{\circ}+\omega^{\prime}
.

The implication of Eqs. (
295
) and (
298
)
is that

V
,
the
same
vector field from the free theory
(\omega^{\circ},H)
,
implements an infinitesimal symmetry transformation
on the interacting theory
(\omega,H)
as well,
yet via the modified Noether charge

Q=Q^{\circ}+Q^{\prime}
in Eq. (
298
):

\displaystyle V\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{Q^{\circ}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{Q}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(299)

Further, suppose the free theory admits a number of symmetry charges forming an algebra.
The above analysis shows that the symmetry actions on the phase space
(as the vector fields)
remain the same
under symmetry-preserving symplectic perturbations.
As a result, the symmetry algebra remains the same
in the interacting theory.

Scalar Particle in Electromagnetism

For a concrete demonstration of the above abstract formalization,
we could revisit the familiar example of the charged scalar particle
described in Sec.
5.1
.

The free theory is defined in Eq. (
100
),
which supposes
the phase space
T^{*}\mathbb{M}

with coordinates
x^{\mu}
and
p_{\mu}
.
The symplectic form is
\omega^{\circ}=dp_{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\mu}
,
while the mass-shell constraint
\phi_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt(\eta^{\mu\nu}p_{\mu}p_{\nu}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}m^{2})
serves as the Hamiltonian.
A well-known fact is that

\displaystyle V\,=\,K^{\mu}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}-p_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\nu}{}_{,\mu}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\mu}}

(300)

generates a symmetry transformation
on
(T^{*}\mathbb{M},\omega^{\circ},\phi_{0})

iff
K^{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\partial/\partial x^{\mu}

is a Killing vector of flat spacetime
\mathbb{M}{\>=\>}(\mathbb{R}^{4},\eta)
.
To elaborate,
the symplectic condition
is trivially satisfied by the ansatz in Eq. (
300
),
identifying the Noether charge as

\displaystyle Q^{\circ}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)\,.

(301)

The Hamiltonian condition
then yields the
Killing equation in flat spacetime,

\displaystyle\pounds_{K}\eta\,=\,0{\quad\iff\quad}K_{\mu,\nu}(x)\,=\,-K_{\nu,\mu}(x)\,.

(302)

Now consider an electromagnetic field strength
F

on flat spacetime
such that

\displaystyle\pounds_{K}F\,=\,0\,.

(303)

Via the Cartan formula
and the closure
dF=0
,
Eq. (
303
) implies the local existence of a scalar field
{\alpha}
such that
Hughston:1972qf

\displaystyle\iota_{K}F\,=\,-d{\alpha}{\quad\iff\quad}K^{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptF_{\mu\nu}(x)\,=\,-{\alpha}_{,\nu}(x)\,.

(304)

Crucially, the condition in Eq. (
303
)
implies that

\omega^{\prime}{\>=\>}qF

(now viewed as a two-form on
\mathds{P}

via the pullback of the bundle projection)
is a symmetry-preserving symplectic perturbation.
Namely, it is easy to show that
\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\omega^{\prime}=0
by
\pounds_{K}F=0
.
According to Eq. (
298
),
the correction to the Noether charge
can be taken as

Q^{\prime}=q\hskip 0.50003pt{\alpha}
,
as

-dQ^{\prime}=\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}=-q\hskip 1.00006ptd{\alpha}
.
Therefore, the exact Noether charge
in the interacting theory is

\displaystyle Q\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)+{\alpha}(x)\,.

(305)

Eq. (
305
) is precisely
the conserved charge of the charged scalar probe

Hughston:1972qf
.

To derive the perhaps more widely-recognized formula

Q=(\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{\mu}+qA_{\mu}(x)})\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)
,
note that the scalar field
{\alpha}
in Eq. (
304
) can be explicitly given as follows
if one is willing to make an explicit gauge choice:

\displaystyle\pounds_{K}A\,=\,0{\quad\implies\quad}{\alpha}(x)\,=\,qA_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)\,.

(306)

Spinning Particles in Electromagnetism

Now we are ready to derive the
universal
(model-ignorant) conserved charges
of massive spinning particles
in electromagnetism.

Suppose any Hamiltonian formulation of a free massive spinning particle
based on a phase space
(\mathds{P},\omega^{\circ})
.
Suppose infinitesimal symmetry
V
exists for this particle.
Our ansatz for the symmetry action on the physical variables is

\displaystyle\begin{split}V[\,{x^{\mu}}\,]\,&=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{Q^{\circ}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,K^{\mu}(x)\,,\\
V[\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}}\,]\,&=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\hat{y}^{\mu}},{Q^{\circ}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,K^{\mu}{}_{,\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\,,\\
V[\,{p_{\mu}}\,]\,&=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{p_{\mu}},{Q^{\circ}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}^{\circ}\,=\,-p_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\nu}{}_{,\mu}(x)\,.\end{split}

(307)

To validate this ansatz,
we apply the two conditions in Eq. (
291
).
First, the symplectic condition is solved by
taking the Noether charge as

\displaystyle Q^{\circ}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu,\nu}(x)\,,

(308)

which reproduces Eq. (
307
) via the
universal Poisson bracket relation in Eq. (
99
)
provided that
K^{\mu}(x)
exhibits linearity:

K^{\mu}{}_{,\rho\sigma}(x)=0
.
Second, the Hamiltonian condition is
satisfied if
K_{\mu,\nu}(x)=-K_{\nu,\mu}(x)
,
as Eq. (
307
) implies that
the symmetry action on the mass-shell constraint

\phi_{0}{\>=\>}\frac{1}{2}\,(p^{2}{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}m^{2})

is
V[\,{\phi_{0}}\,]=-p_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\nu}{}_{,\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\mu}
.
Crucially, since
Q^{\circ}
in Eq. (
308
) is composed solely of the physical variables
(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},p_{\mu})
,
it must Poisson-commute with any gauge constraints
of the model.
Consequently, the Hamiltonian action of Eq. (
308
)
passes both the symplectic and Hamiltonian tests of symmetry in Eq. (
291
).

Note that this fact could be shown
without writing down
an explicit formula for
V

(which will depend on the microscopic implementations, i.e., the details of the model).

It follows that
the symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}{\>=\>}qF

due to the universal minimal coupling recipe
in Eq. (
111
)
is symmetry-preserving
as long as the electromagnetic background
F

exhibits symmetry à la Eq. (
303
).
Since Eq. (
307
) implies

\iota_{V}dx^{\mu}=V[\,{x^{\mu}}\,]=K^{\mu}(x)
,
we have
(with abuse of notation regarding the pullbacks due to bundle projection)

\displaystyle\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}\,=\,d\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{V}\omega^{\prime}\,

\displaystyle=\,q\hskip 1.00006ptd\Big(\,{K^{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptF_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\nu}}\,\Big)\,=\,q\hskip 1.00006ptd\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{K}F\,=\,q\hskip 1.00006pt\pounds_{K}F\,=\,0\,.

(309)

Therefore, the same vector field
V

still defines a symmetry in the interacting theory.

It remains to find the Noether charge.
Since

-dQ^{\prime}=\iota_{V}\hskip 0.50003pt\omega^{\prime}=q\hskip 1.00006pt\iota_{K}F=-q\hskip 1.00006ptd{\alpha}
,
so we can take
Q^{\prime}=q\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}(x)
.
Thus,
we conclude that
minimally coupled
massive spinning particles
in electromagnetism
universally exhibits the conserved charge

\displaystyle Q\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)-\tfrac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu,\nu}(x)+q\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}(x)\,,

(310)

where the gauge choice
\pounds_{K}A=0

will take
{\alpha}(x)=A_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)
.

To reiterate,
the Poisson commutativity of
Eq. (
310
)
with the various constraints of the particle
is automatic
since the infinitesimal symmetry transformation
V
is left unchanged.
Still, it is a nice and interesting exercise to
reconfirm
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{H},{Q}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=0

by direct computation,
by using the exact universal
EoM derived in Eq. (
114
),
for instance.

Incorporating the multipolar couplings is not difficult.
For instance,
the non-minimal coupling
in Eq. (
117
)
adds

\theta^{\prime}=(g/2)\hskip 1.00006ptq\,{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)

\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\nu}

on the symplectic potential.
Computation using Eq. (
307
) shows that

\pounds_{V}\hskip 0.24994pt\theta^{\prime}=0

by
\pounds_{K}{*}F=0
.
Here,
\pounds_{K}
preserves the Hodge star
because it preserves the flat metric.
This shows that
\theta^{\prime}
is a symmetry-preserving perturbation on the symplectic potential.
Based on our earlier discussion around Eq. (
294
),
this implies that the universal conserved charge
with the generic gyromagnetic ratio
g
is

\displaystyle Q\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu,\nu}(x)+q\hskip 1.00006pt{\alpha}(x)+\frac{g}{2}\>q\,{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\nu}(x)\,.

(311)

Spinning Particles in Gravity

Lastly, we concern gravitational backgrounds
with Killing vectors.
To expedite our discussion,
let us directly jump to spinning particles.

Suppose a curved spacetime
\mathcal{M}=(\mathbb{R}^{4},g)

admits a Killing vector
K=K^{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\partial/\partial x^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle K_{\mu;\nu}(x)\,=\,-K_{\nu;\mu}(x){\quad\implies\quad}K_{\mu;\nu;\rho}(x)\,=\,-K_{\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\sigma}{}_{\rho\mu\nu}(x)\,.

(312)

Let
(\mathcal{P},\omega)
be the phase space of any massive spinning particle put in this curved spacetime,
minimally coupled
so that
\omega=\omega^{\bullet}+\omega^{\prime}

and
\omega^{\prime}=p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}

as postulated in Eqs. (
153
) and (
154
).
The ansatz for the associated conserved charge is

\displaystyle Q\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)-\tfrac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu;\nu}(x)\,,

(313)

which arises by covariantizing Eq. (
308
).
The differential
dQ{\>=\>}DQ
of Eq. (
313
) splits into two parts as

dQ=(dQ)^{\bullet}+(dQ)^{\prime}
, where

\displaystyle(dQ)^{\bullet}\,

\displaystyle=\,Dp_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)+p_{\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptK^{\nu}{}_{;\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}-\tfrac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptD(\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma})\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu;\nu}(x)\,,

(314a)

\displaystyle(dQ)^{\prime}\,

\displaystyle=\,dx^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 0.50003ptK^{\nu}(x)\,.

(314b)

The first part,
Eq. (
314a
),
would directly follow by covariantizing
the free theory’s differential
dQ^{\circ}

for Eq. (
308
).
The second part,
Eq. (
314b
),
is a curvature effect.
We have used the well-known identity in Eq. (
312
) when deriving Eq. (
314b
).

With this understanding, the straightforward covariantization
of the vector field
V
in Eq. (
307
)
would be

\mathcal{V}=\Pi^{\bullet}({\,\,\,\,\,},(dQ)^{\bullet})
.
In particular,
\mathcal{V}
coincides with
V

in the vanishing curvature limit
such that
(\mathcal{P},\omega)\to(\mathds{P},\omega^{\circ})
.

Our task now is to prove the following proposition
as the generalization of Eq. (
299
)
for gravitational interactions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\,=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{(dQ)^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{dQ}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,.

(315)

That is, tidal effects do not alter the symmetry action
\mathcal{V}
on the general-relativistic phase space
\mathcal{P}
.
Note that the first expression of
\mathcal{V}
in Eq. (
315
)
completely characterizes the symmetry action on the universal variables
via covariantization of Eq. (
307
),
which should be clear from Eq. (
314a
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathcal{V}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,&=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{(dQ)^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,K^{\mu}(x)\,,\\
\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{\mu}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathcal{V}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,&=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{D\hat{y}^{\mu}},{(dQ)^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,K^{\mu}{}_{;\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\,,\\
\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{\mu}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathcal{V}}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,&=\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{Dp_{\mu}},{(dQ)^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,=\,-p_{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\nu}{}_{;\mu}(x)\,.\end{split}

(316)

The proof proceeds by
computing the difference between the two expressions in Eq. (
315
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\quad\mathit{\Delta}\mathcal{V}\,:=\,{}&{}{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{dQ}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,-\,{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{(dQ)^{\bullet}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\\
\,=\,{}&{}{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{(dQ)^{\prime}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}-{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{dx^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\nu}},{dQ}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,,\\
\,=\,{}&{}{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{dx^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 0.50003pt\Big(\,{K^{\nu}(x)-{\Pi\hskip 0.12497pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\nu}},{dQ}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,=\,{}&{}{-{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\,\,\,\,\,}},{dx^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\sigma\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\nu}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathit{\Delta}\mathcal{V}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle}\,.\kern-10.00002pt\end{split}

(317)

The second line follows by using the identity in Eq. (
149
).
The third line arises by plugging in Eq. (
314b
).
The fourth line is due to the first line of Eq. (
316
).
Clearly, Eq. (
317
) implies

\displaystyle\bigg(\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\delta^{\mu}{}_{\sigma}+{\Pi^{\bullet}\hskip-0.50003pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\mu}},{dx^{\rho}}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\hskip 1.00006pt\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{p_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\lambda}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\big\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\sigma}},\hskip 0.50003pt{\mathit{\Delta}\mathcal{V}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\big\rangle\,=\,0\,.

(318)

The matrix described in Eq. (
318
)
is, at least for mildly curved spacetimes,
invertible.
Hence we conclude that
\langle\hskip 0.50003pt{dx^{\sigma},\mathit{\Delta}\mathcal{V}\hskip 0.50003pt}\hskip 0.50003pt\rangle=0
,
which implies
\mathit{\Delta}\mathcal{V}=0
through Eq. (
317
).

It is left as an exercise to develop this gymnastics
in the presence of multipolar couplings.
In particular,
the quadrupole coupling in Eq. (
161
)
adds

\theta^{\prime}=-(C_{2}/2)\,p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)

\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}dx^{\sigma}

on the symplectic potential,
in which case the Noether charge is

\displaystyle Q\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\mu}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptK_{\mu;\nu}(x)-\frac{C_{2}}{2!}\,p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{\sigma}(x)\,.

(319)

Model-Specific Derivations

Of course, the above discussions become much more transparent
if one works in the model-specific fashion.
For example, the symmetry action in the spinor oscillator model is

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\,

\displaystyle=\,K^{m}(x)\,\tilde{E}_{m}-p_{n}\hskip 1.00006ptK^{n}{}_{;m}(x)\,\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{m}}+\frac{1}{2}\>K_{m;n}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma^{mn})_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt\zeta_{\beta}\>\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta_{\alpha}}\,,

(320)

and contracting Eq. (
320
)
with Eq. (
267
)
immediately yields Eq. (
313
).

It should be interesting to explore symmetries due to Killing or Killing-Yano tensors
in this universal framework
and understand whether
they obey
Eqs. (
299
) and (
315
).

9.2
Post-Minkowskian Expansion

The PM expansion seeks to
solve the dynamics of two gravitationally interacting and radiating
relativistic bodies
while maintaining the global Poincaré symmetry
of the flat Minkowski background manifestly.
Its modern driving force has been
scattering amplitudes, effective field theory, and worldline methods

Damour:2016gwp
;
Cheung:2018wkq
;
Cristofoli:2019neg
;
Bjerrum-Bohr:2021din
;
Kalin:2020mvi
;
Kalin:2020fhe
;
Mogull:2020sak
;
Jakobsen:2021smu
;
Bjerrum-Bohr:2022blt
;
Vines:2017hyw
.
In this subsection, we discuss
the PM expansion for spinning bodies
from the perspectives of this work.

Classical Eikonal for Universal Observables

The ultimate objective of a classical mechanics problem
is to determine the final state from the initial state
by solving the EoM.
Recently, a number of works

Gonzo:2024zxo
;
Kim:2024svw
;
Kim:2025hpn
;
Alessio:2025flu
;
Kim:2025olv
;
Kim:2025gis
;
Kim:2025sey

have identified an efficient currency for classical mechanics
that is well-suited for in-in boundary value problems:
the Magnusian.
The Magnusian is the generator of the symplectomorphism
mapping the initial phase space to the final phase space
in Hamiltonian formulation.
Its historical roots can be traced back to
Refs.
hunziker1968s
;
simon1971wave
;
herbst1974classical
;
sokolov1979classical
;
narnhofer1981canonical
;
thirring1981classical
;
osborn1980levinson
;
levinson1949uniqueness
,
while
the name Magnusian derives from the fact that
it is computed by the Magnus series
magnus1954exponential

(to be compared with the Dyson series).
In the scattering context, the Magnusian is referred to as classical eikonal or scattering generator.
In a rigorous fashion,
it is established that
the classical eikonal
defines the classical limit of the log of the S-matrix

Kim:2025ebl
.
The classical eikonal is a single scalar function on the asymptotic (free) phase space
that encapsulates all classical scattering observables.

In the worldline framework
known as worldline quantum field theory (WQFT)

Mogull:2020sak
;
Jakobsen:2021smu
,
the diagrammatic computations encode
Berends-Giele recursion for solving EoM;
see Refs.
Kim:2024grz
;
Hoogeveen:2025tew
for examples with spinning particles.
The Magnus series formula for classical eikonal
describes nested Poisson/Peierls brackets of
interaction vertices at different times,
producing a weighted sum of WQFT diagrams composed of retarded propagators.

Regarding universality,
any two equivalent spinning particle models
should predict the same relation between
the initial and final universal observables,
thus defining Magnusians
that generate the same effect on the
(x,\hat{y},p)
space.
At intermediate steps during computations, however,
the four models will produce
totally different diagrammatic expansions in WQFT.
A detailed comparison on the efficiency of their WQFT diagrammatics
would be interesting,
say, in terms of the total number of diagrams for an observable.

In fact,
one could also directly study the model-independent part of the time evolution
by focusing on the EoM of
(x,\hat{y},p)
:
an approach initiated in Ref.
Kim:2024grz
.
This method approaches the universal observables directly
and would be preferred over
model-specific or gauge-dependent methods.
The universality in the classical theory
will be ensured as long as
all interaction vertices are expressed in terms of
(x,\hat{y},p)
.
A possible technicality is that
the nonlinear nature of the Poisson algebra
(Eq. (
1
) or Eq. (
5
))
may induce extra vertices
if the nested bracket formulae are applied in a straightforward fashion.

It is also worth emphasizing that,
for gravitational interactions,
the intermediate time evolution describes
the
general-relativistic

(x,\hat{y},p)
variables in the bulk.
However, the classical eikonal for scattering in asymptotically flat spacetimes
act on the
flat

(x,\hat{y},p)
space,
as it connects between
the initial and final asymptotic (free) phase spaces
(cf. Ref.
sst-asym
).

Model-Specific Features

We now turn to
the effects of model-specific features
on PM expansion.
First of all,
models with less
redundancy
may be argued as preferable.
In this perspective,
the spinor oscillator model is identified as the most ideal,
while the spherical top is by no means efficient.
It carries the angle (body frame) variables that do not affect the universal observables
(x,\hat{y},p)

as long as the interactions are spin-magnitude preserving;
similar comments apply to the vector oscillator and massive twistor models as well.

Second of all,
there is a discrepancy
between
linear
models and
nonlinear
models.
Take the spherical top model,
for instance,
which describes a sigma model to a nonlinear target space
(the cotangent bundle of Poincaré group).
As a result,
its symplectic potential in Eq. (
49
)
is cubic due to the spin term,
so the worldline perturbation theory is already complicated
at the level of
free theory
.
Remarkably,
the massive twistor model
reformulates the spherical top
as a sigma model to a linear target space (
{\sim\,}\mathbb{C}^{8}
)
in which the symplectic potential
takes the canonical, bilinear form as shown in Eq. (
64
)
(cf. Darboux theorem
darboux1882probleme
).
Consequently, the diagrammatics of the massive twistor model
is much simpler than the spherical top model.
Similar analysis applies to the spinor and vector oscillators as well;
the former is nonlinear, while the latter is linear.

Third of all,
when one aims to describe spinning
black holes
,
there exists a distinction between
the spinorial models and the vectorial models.
As is glimpsed in Eqs. (
40
) and (
70
)
and will be explicated in Sec.
9.5
,
the spinorial models feature descriptions
in which
the so-called spinspacetime coordinates
sst-asym

can be taken as basic variables,
which can be important for comprehending and implementing the Newman-Janis shift.
For instance, the one-line reformulation of the universal Poisson bracket relation in Eq. (
345
)
is immediate in the massive twistor model,
although typically obscured.

Last but not least, one shall also pay attention to the Lagrange multipliers.
In the vector oscillator model,
the Lagrange multiplier terms
produce vertex factors that one cannot read off directly from the Lagrangian.
The WQFT rule of the spinor oscillator model is not affected by constraints, so the bare Feynman rules are sufficient for computing observables.

Further Comments

We close this subsection with some comments on Ref.
Hoogeveen:2025tew
, where a fully systematic WQFT expansion based on the vector oscillator model is provided. A similar expansion based on our presentation of the vector oscillator model will eventually produce the same set of observables, but the intermediate steps may look rather different.

First, we are using symplectic perturbation to implement nonminimal couplings while Ref.
Hoogeveen:2025tew
uses Hamiltonian perturbation to achieve the same effect. The two approaches could be related to by a noncanonical change of variables.
Second, while in our approach the SSC is imposed from the outset nonperturbatively, Ref.
Hoogeveen:2025tew
verifies the preservation of the covariant SSC perturbatively, order by order in spin.
Finally, Ref.
Hoogeveen:2025tew
makes an impression that the WQFT prefers the Lagrangian (second-order) formulation while the constraint analysis is usually done in the Hamiltonian (first-order) formulation.
However, as elucidated in Refs.
Gonzo:2024zxo
;
Kim:2024svw
;
Kim:2025hpn
;
Alessio:2025flu
;
Kim:2025olv
;
Kim:2025gis
;
Kim:2025sey
,
the Hamiltonian formulation is both conceptually more suitable and computationally more efficient
than the Lagrangian formulation when it comes to computing the classical eikonal (Magnusian).

9.3
Post-Newtonian Expansion

In this subsection,
we explicate the implications of our work for
the PN effective field theory.
The plan is to revisit
the leading order spin-orbit coupling
in a streamlined fashion
and comment on the necessary role of the
fictitious vertices
which the rigorous construction of the interacting spherical top in Sec.
7
stipulates.
Within this subsection, we switch to the usual notation:

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}{}_{A},p_{\mu^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt\nu^{\prime}})\to(x^{\mu},\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{A},p_{\mu},S_{\mu\nu})
.

Canonical Gauge

The PN expansion is conventionally performed in a fixed lab frame,
in which case
it is natural to employ the canonical (Pryce-Newton-Wigner) spin gauge
described in Eq. (
56
).
Below, we record more details on the canonical spin gauge.
13
13

13

The comparison between the canonical and covariant spin gauges is detailed in
Appendix A of Ref.
Lee:2023nkx
.

The time axis of the lab frame is characterized by
a unit timelike four-vector
l^{\mu}=(1,\vec{0})
.
The gauge-fixing functions for the translational and rotational sectors are

\displaystyle\chi^{0}\,=\,-\frac{1}{m}\>l_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptx^{\mu}\,,\quad\chi^{a}\,=\,l_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu a}\,,

(321)

respectively.
In particular,
the spin gauge-fixing condition
\chi^{a}=0
sets that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{0}\,=\,l^{\mu}\,,\quad\Big(\,{p^{\mu}+m\hskip 1.00006ptl^{\mu}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptS_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\nu}{}_{a}\,=\,0\,.

(322)

After the
1+3
decomposition,
the second equation in Eq. (
322
) describes

\displaystyle(E+m)\hskip 1.00006ptS_{0i}+p^{j}S_{ji}\,=\,0{\quad\iff\quad}S_{0i}\,=\,\frac{S_{ij}\hskip 0.50003ptp^{j}}{E+m}\,=\,-\frac{(\vec{S}\times\vec{p})_{i}}{E+m}\,,

(323)

where we have defined

\displaystyle S_{i}\,:=\,\frac{1}{2}\>\varepsilon_{ijk}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{jk}\,,\quad E:=\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}\,.

(324)

Similarly,
the temporal and spatial components of the vector
b^{\mu}
in Eq. (
183
) are

\displaystyle b^{0}\,=\,\frac{1}{m^{2}}\>S^{0i}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{i}\,=\,0\,,\quad b^{i}\,=\,\frac{1}{m^{2}}\>\Big(\,{S^{i0}p_{0}+S^{ij}\hskip 0.24994ptp_{j}}\,\Big)\,=\,-\frac{(\vec{S}\times\vec{p})^{i}}{m(E+m)}\,.

(325)

It could be also helpful to
note that the physical spin length vector
\hat{y}^{\mu}

is given by

Lee:2023nkx

\displaystyle-\hat{y}^{0}\,=\,\frac{\vec{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot}\vec{S}}{m}\,,\quad-\hat{y}^{i}\,=\,\frac{S^{i}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{p^{i}\,(\vec{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot}\vec{S})}{m^{2}(E+m)}\,.

(326)

1+3 Decomposition in Free Theory

Next, we review the derivation of the “non-covariant” action
at the level of free theory.

The gauge-fixing function
\chi^{0}
in Eq. (
321
)
essentially states that
the worldline parameter
will be taken as the lab time coordinate
x^{0}=t
.
By resolving the mass-shell constraint as well,
the symplectic potential of the translational sector
is boiled down to

\displaystyle p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}\,=\,p_{i}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}-\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006ptdt\,,

(327)

which identifies
\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}
as the Hamiltonian.

For the rotational sector,
the
1{\hskip 1.00006pt+\hskip 1.00006pt}3
decomposition describes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\mu\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{\mu}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptd\hskip-0.50003pt\Lambda^{\nu A}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\,S_{ij}\hskip 1.00006pt\Lambda^{i}{}_{A}\hskip 1.00006ptd\hskip-0.50003pt\Lambda^{jA}\,,

(328)

as the canonical spin gauge-fixing in Eq. (
322
) mandates that the body frame can only exhibit
\mathrm{SO}(3)
rotations transverse to the time axis
l^{\mu}
.
Hence Eq. (
328
) is immediate
despite the complicated expression for
S_{0i}
in Eq. (
323
).

With these understandings,
the “non-covariant” action is found as

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\circ}\,=\,\int dt\,\,\bigg(\,{p_{i}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{i}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2}\>S_{ij}\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega^{ij}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}}\,\bigg)\qquad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{where}}}}}}\qquad\Omega^{ij}\,:=\,\Lambda^{i}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\Lambda}^{ja}\,.

(329)

In this subsection, the overdot denotes
d/dt
.
It is instructive to derive the free EoM
from the non-covariant action in Eq. (
329
):

\dot{x}^{i}=p^{i}\hskip-1.00006pt/E
,

\dot{p}_{i}=0
,

\dot{S}_{ij}=0
,
and
\Omega_{ij}\propto S_{ij}
.

1+3 Decomposition in Interacting Theory

Now we incorporate the gravitational interactions.
In the PN effective field theory formalism
pioneered by Levi and Steinhoff
Levi:2015msa
,
the metric is parametrized by
the temporal Kaluza-Klein variables
put forward by Kol and Smolkin
Kol:2007bc
:

\displaystyle ds^{2}\>=\>-{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{2\phi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{dt-A_{i}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}}\,\Big)^{2}+{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{-2\phi}\,\sigma_{ij}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}\hskip 0.50003ptdx^{j}\,.

(330a)

The effects of
\sigma_{ij}
can be safely ignored
at the lowest orders in
v^{i}\sim\dot{x}^{i}
.
Hence, for the purposes of our discussion, it suffices to take

\displaystyle ds^{2}\>\approx\>-{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{2\phi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{dt-A_{i}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}}\,\Big)^{2}+{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{-2\phi}\,d\vec{x}^{2}\,,

(330b)

together with the conditions

\dot{\phi}\approx 0
and
\dot{A}_{i}\approx 0
.

Consequently,
the
1+3
decomposition of the vierbein will be

\displaystyle e^{0}\,=\,{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{\phi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{dt-A_{i}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}}\,\Big)\,,\quad e^{i}\,=\,{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{-\phi}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{i}\,.

(331a)

Computation shows that the spin connection is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma^{0}{}_{i}\,&=\,{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{\phi}\hskip 1.00006pt(\partial_{i}\phi)\hskip 1.00006pte^{0}-\frac{1}{2}\>{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{3\phi}\hskip 1.00006ptF_{ij}\hskip 1.00006pte^{j}\,,\\
\gamma_{ij}\,&=\,{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{\phi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{(\partial_{i}\phi)\hskip 1.00006pte_{j}-(\partial_{j}\phi)\hskip 1.00006pte_{i}}\,\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\>{\scalebox{1.025}[1]{$\mathrm{e}$}}^{3\phi}\hskip 1.00006ptF_{ij}\hskip 1.00006pte^{0}\,,\end{split}

(331b)

where
F_{ij}=\partial_{i}A_{j}-\partial_{j}A_{i}
denotes the field strength for the “graviphoton” field.

Based on Eq. (
331a
),
the translational sector
of the gravity-coupled symplectic potential
is boiled down to

\displaystyle\begin{split}p_{m}\hskip 1.00006pte^{m}{}_{\mu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}\,&\approx\,\vec{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}d\vec{x}\,-\,\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}\>(1+\phi)\Big(\,{dt-\vec{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}d\vec{x}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\,&\approx\,\Big(\,{\vec{p}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}d\vec{x}-\sqrt{\vec{p}^{2}+m^{2}}\>dt}\,\Big)+\Big(\,{-m\hskip 1.00006pt\phi+m\hskip 0.50003pt\vec{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}d\vec{x}}\,\Big)\,,\end{split}

(332)

where we have consistently ignored
terms irrelevant for the leading-order calculation.
Eq. (
332
) identifies
the “point-particle part” of the interaction action as

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\smash{\text{mass}}}\,=\,\int dt\,\,\bigg(\,{-m\hskip 1.00006pt\phi+m\hskip 0.50003pt\vec{A}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\dot{\vec{x}}}\,\bigg)\,.
