JHK would like to thank Thibault Damour for
stimulating conversations on spinning particle phase spaces
and sharing his insightful and critical views.
JHK is supported by the Department of Energy (Grant No. DE-SC0011632) and by the Walter Burke Institute for Theoretical Physics.
The work of
SL is supported by National Research Foundation of Korea (NRF) grants,
NRF-2023-K2A9A1A0609593811 and NRF RS-2024-00351197, as well as KIAS grant PG006002.

Appendix A
Symplectic Quotient and Dirac bracket

Our expositions in the main text
rely quite heavily on
the languages of symplectic quotient and Dirac bracket.
Therefore it is the duty of this appendix
to provide a physicist-friendly introduction to those concepts.

What is Symplectic Quotient?

In the mathematics literature,
the idea of symplectic quotient
was systematized
by Marsden and Weinstein

marsden1974reduction
,
and also Meyer
meyer1973symmetries
;
see Ref.
marsden2001comments
for a historical account.
Suppose a Lie group
G
acts on
a symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega)

by Hamiltonian actions.
The momentum map
souriau1970structure

\phi:\mathcal{P}{\>\to\>}\mathfrak{g}^{*}

is a dual Lie algebra valued function
defining the generators of the
G
-action.
The definition of the symplectic quotient reads

marsden1974reduction

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G\,=\,\phi^{-1}(0)/G\,.

(365)

With Eq. (
365
), the following facts hold
marsden1974reduction
;
meyer1973symmetries
.
First,

\pi:\phi^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G

describes a principal
G
-bundle.
Second,

\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G
is a symplectic manifold
by a unique symplectic form,
whose pullback by
\pi

is the pullback of
\omega

by the embedding
\phi^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{P}
.

What does Eq. (
365
) mean, explicitly?
To this end, let
X^{I}
be local coordinates on
\mathcal{P}
,
where
I,J,K,L,\cdots\in\{1,{\cdots},\dim\mathcal{P}\}
.
The momentum map is essentially
a set of functions
\phi_{a}(X)
,
where
a,b,c,d,\cdots\in\{1,{\cdots},\dim\mathfrak{g}\}

are the Lie algebra indices.
As is suggested by the very name “momentum map,”

\phi_{a}(X)
are literally the generalized momenta for the
G
-action.
Namely,
the Hamiltonian vector fields of
\phi_{a}(X)

generate the
G
-transformations on
\mathcal{P}
,
so
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=\phi_{c}\hskip 1.00006ptf^{c}{}_{ab}
.
The formula in Eq. (
365
) then states that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G\,\,=\,\,\Big\{\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006pt{X^{I}\hskip-1.00006pt{\>\in\>}\mathcal{P}\hskip 1.00006pt\,\Big|\,\hskip 0.50003pt\hskip 1.00006pt\phi_{a}(X)=0}\hskip 1.00006pt\,\Big\}\hskip 0.50003pt\Big/G\,,

(366)

which represents the ubiquitous pattern
described in the main text:
see Eqs. (
11
), (
54
), and (
76
),
for instance.
That is,

\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G
is the constrained phase space in
\mathcal{P}

that arises by
fixing the generalized momenta as
\phi_{a}=0

and

performing a quotient by the group action generated by
\phi_{a}
.
The dimension of the symplectic quotient is

\displaystyle\dim(\mathcal{P}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G)\,\,=\,\,\dim\mathcal{P}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt2\hskip 1.00006pt\dim G\,.

(367)

Clearly, the dimension of a symplectic quotient
is always even.

What is Dirac Bracket?

Meanwhile, the theory of constrained Hamiltonian mechanics
was developed by
Dirac
Dirac:1950pj
;
dirac1964lectures

and is well-reviewed in the book
Henneaux:1992ig
.

The gist of the Dirac bracket
is really simple.
Let
(\mathcal{P}_{2n},\omega_{2n})
be a
2n
-dimensional symplectic manifold.
Suppose an embedding
i:\mathcal{P}_{2m}\to\mathcal{P}_{2n}
of a
2m
-dimensional submanifold,
defined as the common zero locus of
functions
\Psi^{\alpha}
on
\mathcal{P}_{2n}

where
{\alpha},{\beta},\cdots{\>\in\>}\{1,{\cdots},2(n{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m)\}
.
If the pullback
\omega_{2m}:=i^{*}\omega_{2n}

is nondegenerate in
\mathcal{P}_{2m}
,
then
(\mathcal{P}_{2m},\omega_{2m})
is a symplectic manifold.
In turn,
\omega_{2m}
can be inverted
within

\mathcal{P}_{2m}

to define a Poisson bivector
\Pi_{2m}
.
The
Dirac bivector

is the pushforward

i_{*}\Pi_{2m}
.
By construction,
this Dirac bivector
defines a Poisson structure on
\mathcal{P}_{2n}
of rank
2m

whose null directions are characterized by the functions
\Psi^{\alpha}
:

(i_{*}\Pi_{2m})({\,\,\,\,\,},d\Psi^{\alpha})=0
.
The Dirac bracket is

\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}:=(i_{*}\Pi_{2m})(df,dg)

for
f,g\in C^{\infty}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathcal{P}_{2n})
.

Explicitly, the formula for the Dirac bracket reads

Dirac:1950pj
;
dirac1964lectures
;
Henneaux:1992ig
.

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,-\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\Psi^{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,(C^{-1})_{{\alpha}{\beta}}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\beta}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,,

(368)

where we assume that
(C^{-1})_{{\alpha}{\beta}}
exists and is well-defined as the inverse of

\displaystyle C^{{\alpha}{\beta}}\,:=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\alpha}},{\Psi^{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,,

(369)

so
C^{{\alpha}{\gamma}}(C^{-1})_{{\gamma}{\beta}}=\delta^{\alpha}{}_{\beta}
.
It should be clear that the right-hand side of Eq. (
368
) is invariant
under any linear transformation on the constraints
\Psi^{\alpha}
.

A derivation of the Dirac bracket
that can be physically insightful
is the following
(see also Appendix A of Ref.
Kim:2021rda
).
Recall how Poisson bracket arises from the variational principle.
Suppose a symplectic manifold
(\mathcal{P},\omega)
.
A generic phase space action
takes the form

\displaystyle\int d\tau\,\,\bigg(\,{\theta_{I}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{I}-g(X)}\,\bigg)\,,

(370)

which defines a sigma model
\mathbb{R}{\>\to\>}(\mathcal{P},\omega)
.
Here,

\theta_{I}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{I}\hskip 1.00006ptd\tau
is the pullback
of a symplectic potential
\theta

such that
d\theta=\omega
,
while
g\in C^{\infty}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathcal{P})
.
By discarding a boundary term,
the bulk variation of Eq. (
370
) is found as

\displaystyle\int d\tau\,\,{{\delta}X}^{I}\,\bigg(\,{\omega_{IJ}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{J}-g_{,I}(X)}\,\bigg)\,,

(371)

from which one derives the Hamiltonian EoM.
The resulting time evolution of a function
f{\>\in\>}C^{\infty}\hskip-0.50003pt(\mathcal{P})

is given by

\displaystyle\dot{f}\,=\,f_{,I}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{I}\,=\,f_{,I}\hskip 1.00006pt\Pi^{IJ}\hskip 0.50003ptg_{,J}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(372)

Here,
\Pi
denotes the pointwise inverse of
\omega
,
so
\omega_{IK}\hskip 1.00006pt\Pi^{KJ}=\delta_{I}{}^{J}
.

The action in Eq. (
370
)
can be thought of as describing
a particle in
\mathcal{P}
.
We can constrain this particle on a submanifold
\Psi^{\alpha}=0

in terms of Lagrange multipliers
\kappa_{\alpha}(\tau)
:

\displaystyle\int d\tau\,\,\bigg(\,{\theta_{I}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{I}-g(X)-\Psi^{\alpha}(X)\,\kappa^{\alpha}}\,\bigg)\,.

(373)

The bulk variation of Eq. (
373
) is

\displaystyle\int d\tau\,\,{{\delta}X}^{I}\,\bigg(\,{\omega_{IJ}(X)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{J}-g_{,I}(X)-\Psi^{\alpha}{}_{,I}(X)\,\kappa^{\alpha}}\,\bigg)-\Psi^{\alpha}(X)\,{\delta}\kappa_{\alpha}\,,

(374)

from which the EoM are found as

\displaystyle\dot{X}^{I}\,=\,\Pi^{IJ}\hskip 0.50003ptg_{,J}+\Pi^{IJ}\hskip 0.50003pt\Psi^{\alpha}{}_{,J}\hskip 1.00006pt\kappa_{\alpha}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{X^{I}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}+\{\hskip 0.50003pt{X^{I}},{\Psi^{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\hskip 1.00006pt\kappa_{\alpha}\,,\quad\Psi^{\alpha}\,=\,0\,.

(375)

As usual, the Lagrange multipliers are determined by imposing
\dot{\Psi}^{\alpha}=0
:

\displaystyle 0\,=\,\dot{\Psi}^{\alpha}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\alpha}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}+\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\alpha}},{\Psi^{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\hskip 1.00006pt\kappa_{\beta}{\quad\implies\quad}\kappa_{\alpha}\,=\,-(C^{-1})_{{\alpha}{\beta}}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\beta}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(376)

By plugging in Eq. (
376
) back to Eq. (
375
),
one finds

\displaystyle\dot{f}\,=\,f_{,I}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{X}^{I}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}+\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\Psi^{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\hskip 1.00006pt\kappa_{\alpha}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}-\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\Psi^{\alpha}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,(C^{-1})_{{\alpha}{\beta}}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\beta}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(377)

Eq. (
377
)
reproduces the right-hand side in Eq. (
368
),
upon restoring the
2n
subscripts.

Successive Reductions

Now suppose a nested embedding of symplectic submanifolds,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}_{2r},\omega_{2r})\>\>\xrightarrow{\>\>\>{i^{\prime}}\>\>\>}\>\>(\mathcal{P}_{2m},\omega_{2m})\>\>\xrightarrow{\>\>\>{i}\>\>\>}\>\>(\mathcal{P}_{2n},\omega_{2n})\,.

(378)

Suppose
\mathcal{P}_{2m}
is defined in
\mathcal{P}_{2n}
by

\Psi^{\alpha}=0
,
while

\mathcal{P}_{2n}
is defined in
\mathcal{P}_{2n}
by

\Psi^{\alpha}=0
and
\Psi^{{\alpha}^{\prime}}=0
.
Here,
{\alpha},{\beta},\cdots
run through
2(n{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m)
integers
while
{\alpha}^{\prime},{\beta}^{\prime},\cdots
run through
2(n^{\prime}{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m^{\prime})
integers.

The rank-
2m
Dirac bivector on
\mathcal{P}_{2n}
is

i_{*}\Pi_{2m}
.
The rank-
2r
Dirac bivector on
\mathcal{P}_{2n}
is

i_{*}i^{\prime}_{*}\Pi_{2r}
.
Suppose
one wants to find
the rank-
2r
Dirac bracket
while the rank-
2m
Dirac bracket is already known.
It can be shown that

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2r}\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}\,-\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\Psi^{{\alpha}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}\,(C^{\prime-1})_{{\alpha}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\beta}^{\prime}}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{{\beta}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}\,,

(379)

where
(C^{\prime-1})_{{\alpha}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\beta}^{\prime}}
is the inverse of

C^{\prime{\alpha}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt{\beta}^{\prime}}:=\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{{\alpha}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}}},{\Psi^{{\beta}{\mathrlap{\smash{{}^{\prime}}}{\hskip 0.40974pt}}\hskip 0.81949pt}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}
.
This “shortcut”
was used in Sec.
3
.

Relation between Symplectic Quotient and Dirac Bracket

Finally,
we explicate the concrete link between
symplectic quotient and Dirac bracket:

\displaystyle\text{Symplectic Quotient}\quad\xleftrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt}\quad\text{Dirac Bracket from First-Class Constraints}\,.

(380)

Below, we study the symplectic quotient
\mathcal{P}_{2n}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G

with
\dim\mathcal{P}_{2n}=2n

and
\dim G=n{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m
.

First of all,
recall that the momentum map
describes generalized momenta
\phi_{a}

that represent the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}

under the Poisson bracket:

\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=\phi_{c}\hskip 1.00006ptf^{c}{}_{ab}
.
In the Dirac terminology

Dirac:1950pj
;
dirac1964lectures
;
Henneaux:1992ig
,
this means that
\phi_{a}=0
are first-class constraints:

\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}
vanish on the common zero locus of
\phi_{a}
.

The symplectic quotient in Eq. (
366
)
is explicitly realized as a symplectic submanifold in
\mathcal{P}

if one picks a particular slice on the
G
-orbit.
Concretely, this is done by a well-behaved set of functions
\chi^{a}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\chi^{a}},{\phi_{b}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}=\delta^{a}{}_{b}

on account of the well-behavedness.

Now take
\Psi^{\alpha}=(\chi^{a},\phi_{a})

as a set of
2(n{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}m)
constraints.
In the Dirac terminology

Dirac:1950pj
;
dirac1964lectures
;
Henneaux:1992ig
,
these
\Psi^{\alpha}
are second-class constraints:
the matrix
C^{{\alpha}{\beta}}=\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\Psi^{\alpha}},{\Psi^{\beta}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}
does not vanish on the common zero locus of
\Psi^{\alpha}

and exhibits full rank
due to the well-behavedness.

Therefore, the formula in Eq. (
368
)
readily applies to define the rank-
2m
Dirac bracket:

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2m}\hskip 1.00006pt\,=\,\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,-\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\chi^{a}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,+\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\chi^{a}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\phi_{a}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{2n}\,.

(381)

Eq. (
381
) describes the pushforward
of a rank-
2m
Poisson bivector
on the explicit realization of
\mathcal{P}_{2n}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G
.
This concretely shows
how the symplectic quotient
\mathcal{P}_{2n}{\kern 0.50003pt/\kern-2.5pt/\kern 1.00006pt}G

is a symplectic manifold,
stated earlier as a mathematical fact.

If
G
is a gauge group,
then
\phi_{a}
are the
gauge generators

while
\chi^{a}
are the
gauge-fixing functions
.
The gauge generators
\phi_{a}
must form first-class constraints
for the closure of the gauge algebra,
while
(\chi^{a},\phi_{a})
must form second-class constraints
to produce a well-defined symplectic structure on the symplectic quotient
as well as a well-defined Dirac bracket on the original phase space.

We clarify that
this paper does not concern the cases
in which gauge redundancies are formulated by
presymplectic manifolds.
The symplectic reduction and Dirac bracket
procedures
in this paper
always start from a nondegenerate symplectic manifold.

Appendix B
Regge Trajectory

In the context of relativistic spinning particle mechanics,
the Regge trajectory
Hanson:1974qy
is a positive function of the spin-squared,

M(\xi){\>>\>}0
,
which is real-analytic in the domain
\xi{\>>\>}0
.
Physically, it describes how the rotational kinetic energy contributes to the rest mass
via the mass-energy equivalence.
On a related note,
it is useful to denote

\displaystyle I(\xi)\,:=\,\frac{1}{2M^{\prime}(\xi)}\,.

(382)

It is not difficult to see that

I(\xi)
in Eq. (
382
) describes the rotational inertia
by considering
the nonrelativistic limit
Kim:2021rda
.

This appendix
explicitly demonstrates that
all of the four models
in Fig.
1

allow for a generic Regge trajectory
in the mass-shell constraint:

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{p^{2}+M^{2}(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2})\hskip-1.00006pt}\,\Big)\,.

(383)

Below, we enumerate
the Dirac brackets
that imposes the generic mass-shell constraint in Eq. (
383
)
in each model.
It suffices to suppose free theory.
For a covariant gauge fixing,
the gauge-fixing function

\chi^{0}

will be always taken as proportional to the dilatation charge.
20
20

20

It is realized in
Ref.
sst-asym
that
the dilatation charge
can be used as a proper time
for the massive particle
(up to customary rescalings by mass).
A fruitful application is provided in its appendix
with a discussion on Poincaré charges for binary systems.

We also describe the free-theory time evolution,
which clarifies the role of the Regge trajectory.

B.1
Vector Oscillator

The Dirac brackets on
\mathds{P}_{12}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}

are shown in Eq. (
30
).

Dirac Brackets

We impose the constraint pair
(\chi^{0},\phi_{0})

on
\mathds{P}_{12}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}

to reach a
10
-dimensional symplectic quotient

\widetilde{\mathds{P}}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}_{10}

(which differs from
\mathds{P}^{\smash{\text{VO}}}_{10}
in Eq. (
29
)).
The resulting Dirac bracket describes

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{10}&\,=\,\frac{J^{\mu\nu}}{M^{2}}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{10}\,=\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{10}&\,=\,-i\,\Big(\,{\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}-\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}_{\nu}}{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{10}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\nu}{}+\frac{1}{MI}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\rho}{}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{10}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{MI}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\rho}{}\,p_{\nu}\,,\end{split}

(384)

where we have denoted

M=M(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2})
and
I=I(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2})

to avoid clutter.

Time Evolution

The free-theory time evolution
due to the generic Hamiltonian constraint in Eq. (
383
) can be found by
using the Dirac brackets in Eq. (
30
):

\dot{f}=\kappa^{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{0}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}
.
For
\kappa^{0}=1/M
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\mu}\,=\,\hat{p}^{\mu}\,,\quad\dot{p}_{\mu}\,=\,0\,,\quad\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}}^{\mu}\,=\,-i\,\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}}{I}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}\,.

(385)

This shows that
the sole effect of the Regge trajectory
on the time evolution
is a
\mathrm{U}(1)
phase rotation of
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}^{\mu}

by the angular frequency

\displaystyle\Omega\,:=\,\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}}{I}{\quad\implies\quad}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}\,=\,I\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega\,.

(386)

Recalling Eq. (
28
),
we see that
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{\bar{\alpha}}}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\bm{{\alpha}}}}

computes the spin magnitude in the vector oscillator model.
Eq. (
386
) identifies the familiar relation
between spin magnitude and angular velocity,
such that
I
indeed describes the rotational inertia.

B.2
Spinor Oscillator

The Poisson brackets on
\mathds{P}_{12}^{\mathrm{SO}}

are shown in Eq. (
35
).

Dirac Brackets

We impose the constraint pair
(\chi^{0},\phi_{0})

on
\mathds{P}_{12}^{\mathrm{SO}}

to reach a
10
-dimensional symplectic quotient

\mathds{P}^{\mathrm{SO}}_{\widetilde{10}}

(which differs from
\mathds{P}^{\mathrm{SO}}_{10}
in Eq. (
44
)).
The resulting Dirac bracket describes

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{x^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\,\widetilde{\,\mathclap{10}\,}\,}\,=\,\frac{J^{\mu\nu}}{M^{2}}\,\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\,\widetilde{\,\mathclap{10}\,}\,}\,=\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\zeta_{\alpha}},{\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\,\widetilde{\,\mathclap{10}\,}\,}\,=\,i\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{{\alpha}{\dot{{\alpha}}}}\,,\\
&\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\zeta_{\alpha}},{x^{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\,\widetilde{\,\mathclap{10}\,}\,}\,=\,\frac{(\sigma^{\mu\nu}\zeta)_{\alpha}}{2M^{2}}\,p_{\nu}+\frac{S_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\zeta_{\beta}}{MI}\,x^{\mu}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\zeta_{\alpha}},{p_{\mu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{\,\widetilde{\,\mathclap{10}\,}\,}\,=\,-\frac{S_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\zeta_{\beta}}{MI}\,p_{\mu}\,,\end{split}

(387)

where

S_{{\alpha}{\beta}}=\frac{i}{4}\hskip 1.00006pt(\zeta_{\alpha}\xi_{\beta}+\xi_{\alpha}\zeta_{\beta})

describes Eq. (
36
) in the spinor notation.

Time Evolution

The free-theory time evolution
due to the generic Hamiltonian constraint in Eq. (
383
) is found by
using the Poisson brackets in Eq. (
35
):

\dot{f}=\kappa^{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{0}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}
.
For
\kappa^{0}=1/M
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\hat{p}^{\mu}\,,\quad\dot{p}_{\mu}\,=\,0\,,\quad\dot{\zeta}_{\alpha}\,=\,\frac{1}{2i}\,\bigg(\,{-\frac{1}{2I}\,\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}\zeta_{\beta}}\,\bigg)\,\zeta_{\alpha}\,.

(388)

This shows that
the sole effect of the Regge trajectory
on the time evolution
is a
\mathrm{U}(1)
phase rotation of
\zeta_{\alpha}

by the angular frequency

\displaystyle\Omega\,:=\,-\frac{1}{2I}\,\bar{\zeta}_{\dot{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}\zeta_{\beta}{\quad\implies\quad}\frac{1}{2}\,\bar{\xi}^{\alpha}\zeta_{\alpha}\,=\,I\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega\,,

(389)

where we have used Eq. (
38
).
Recalling Eq. (
43
),
we see that
\bar{\xi}^{\alpha}\zeta_{\alpha}/2

computes the spin magnitude in the spinor oscillator model.
Therefore,
we again identify the familiar relation between
spin magnitude and angular velocity
such that
I
is the rotational inertia.

Note that we peel off
1/2i
in Eq. (
388
)
(instead of
1/i
in Eq. (
385
))
because the phase of a spinor rotates in a half speed:
the double cover.

B.3
Spherical Top

Dirac Brackets

First,
imposing
the constraints

\phi_{a}{\>=\>}0
and
\chi^{a}{\>=\>}0

identifies
the
14
-dimensional physical phase space

\mathds{P}_{14}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
:
Its Poisson structure
is characterized by the Dirac bracket
whose nonzero components are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\frac{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}}{-p^{2}}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,2\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\smash{[\rho}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\sigma]}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\Big(\,{-2\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{[\rho}\hat{\eta}_{\sigma]\nu}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,=\,\Big(\,{-4\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{[\kappa}{}_{[\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\eta}_{\nu][\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\lambda]}{}_{\sigma]}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa\lambda}\,,\end{split}

(390)

where
\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}{\>:=\>}\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}-p^{\mu}p_{\nu}/p^{2}

and
\hat{\eta}_{\mu\nu}{\>:=\>}\eta_{\mu\nu}-p_{\mu}p_{\nu}/p^{2}
.

Second,
imposing
the constraints

\phi_{0}{\>=\>}0
and
\chi^{0}{\>=\>}0

identifies
the
12
-dimensional symplectic submanifold

\mathds{P}_{12}^{\smash{\text{ST}}}
.
Its Poisson structure
is characterized by the Dirac bracket
whose nonzero components are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{J^{\mu\nu}}{M^{2}}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,-\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,2\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\smash{[\rho}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\smash{\sigma]}\vphantom{{\beta}}}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\Big(\,{-2\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{[\rho}\hat{\eta}_{\sigma]\nu}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}\,,\\
&\phantom{{}\,,\quad{}}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\rho\sigma}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\Big(\,{-4\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{[\kappa}{}_{[\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\eta}_{\nu][\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{\delta}^{\lambda]}{}_{\sigma]}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\kappa\lambda}\,,\end{split}

(391a)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{MI}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho}{}_{a}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{MI}\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu}{}_{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho}{}_{a}\,p_{\nu}\,,\end{split}

(391b)

where
M=M(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2})
and
I=I(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2}):=(\hskip 0.24994pt2M^{\prime}\hskip-0.50003pt(-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2}))^{-1}

are
functions
of
-p^{2}\hat{y}^{2}
.
By taking the nonrelativistic limit,
one sees that
I
describes the rotational inertia.

It suffices to consider the spatial part
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}
of the body frame
in the above Dirac brackets,
as the boost components are removed.
In the same way,
it is more helpful to examine
the pseudovector
\hat{y}^{\mu}

rather than the bulky six-component variable
{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}_{\mu\nu}
.
Straightforward algebra shows that
Eq. (
390
)
is rewritten in the

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a},p_{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu})
basis as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\hat{y}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}p^{\nu}+p^{\mu}\hat{y}^{\nu}}\,\Big)\,,\\[1.43994pt]
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\delta^{\mu}{}_{\nu}\,,\\[1.43994pt]
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho\sigma}\,p^{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\sigma}{}_{a}\end{split}

(392)

Similarly,
Eq. (
391
)
is rewritten in the

({\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu},{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a},p_{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu})
basis as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{J^{\mu\nu}}{M^{2}}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,\hat{y}^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\mu}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}&\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\hat{y}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\,,\end{split}

(393a)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,p^{\mu}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\nu}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{MI}\,(\varepsilon^{\mu}{}_{\rho\kappa\lambda}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda})\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}^{\nu}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu}{}_{\rho\sigma}\,p^{\rho}\hskip 0.50003pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\sigma}{}_{a}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}},{p_{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,-\frac{1}{MI}\,(\varepsilon^{\mu}{}_{\rho\kappa\lambda}\hskip 1.00006pt{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\rho}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda})\,p_{\nu}\,.\end{split}

(393b)

Time Evolution

The free-theory time evolution
due to Eq. (
383
) follows by
using the Dirac brackets in Eq. (
58
):

\dot{f}=\kappa^{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{0}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}
.
For
\kappa^{0}=1/M
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{x}}}^{\mu}\,=\,\hat{p}^{\mu}\,,\quad\dot{p}_{\mu}\,=\,0\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}}{}^{\nu a}\,=\,\frac{1}{I}\>{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}\,.

(394)

This identifies
a proportionality between
spin and angular velocity
such that
I
describes an isotropic rotational inertia
(hence the name
spherical
top):

\displaystyle\Omega^{\mu\nu}\,:=\,{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\mu}{}_{a}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{\Lambda}}}{}^{\nu a}{\quad\implies\quad}{\color[rgb]{0.8789,0.1602,0.5313}\hskip 0.50003pt\mathbf{S}}^{\mu\nu}\,=\,I\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega^{\mu\nu}\,.

(395)

B.4
Massive Twistor

Dirac Brackets

The Poisson structure of
\mathds{P}_{14}^{\mathrm{MT}}
in the hybrid basis

(x^{\mu},\hat{y}^{\mu},\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I})
is specified by the following nonzero components,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{x^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\hat{y}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{-p^{2}}\,\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}p^{\nu}+p^{\mu}\hat{y}^{\nu}}\,\Big)\,,\\[1.43994pt]
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{x^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{1}{2}\,\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}I}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{\hat{y}^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}\,&=\,\frac{i}{2}\,\bigg(\,{\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}I}-\frac{1}{2}\,\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1}\lambda^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\,\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(396)

Imposing the constraints
\phi_{0}=0
and
\chi^{0}=0
leads us to
\mathds{P}_{12}^{\mathrm{MT}}
with

\displaystyle\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{x^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{J^{\mu\nu}}{M^{2}}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\hat{y}^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\sigma}\,,\quad\{\hskip 0.50003pt{x^{\mu}},{\hat{y}^{\nu}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,=\,\frac{1}{M^{2}}\,\hat{y}^{\mu}p^{\nu}\,,

(397a)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{x^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}I}-\frac{1}{2}\,\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1}\lambda^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\,\Big)-\frac{i}{2MI}\,(\lambda W)_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,x^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{y^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,\frac{i}{2}\,\delta_{\alpha}{}^{\beta}\hskip 1.00006pt(\bar{\lambda}^{-1})^{{\dot{{\beta}}}I}-\frac{i}{2MI}\,(\lambda W)_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,y^{{\dot{{\beta}}}{\beta}}\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{\lambda_{\beta}{}^{J}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,\frac{i}{4MI}\,\Big(\,{(\lambda W)_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,\lambda_{\beta}{}^{J}-\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,(\lambda W)_{\beta}{}^{J}}\,\Big)\,,\\
\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}},{\bar{\lambda}_{\smash{J{\dot{{\beta}}}}\vphantom{{\beta}}}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{12}\,&=\,\frac{i}{4MI}\,\Big(\,{(\lambda W)_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,\bar{\lambda}_{\smash{J{\dot{{\beta}}}}\vphantom{{\beta}}}+\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,(W\bar{\lambda})_{\smash{J{\dot{{\beta}}}}\vphantom{{\beta}}}}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(397b)

Here,

W_{I}{}^{J}

is an
\mathrm{SU}(2)
matrix
encoding the body-frame components
W^{a}
of the spin pseudovector:

\displaystyle W^{a}\,:=\,-|p|\,(\hat{y}{\hskip 1.00006pt\cdot\hskip 1.00006pt}\Lambda^{a})\,,\quad W_{I}{}^{J}\,=\,W^{a}\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma_{a})_{I}{}^{J}\,.

(398)

Time Evolution

The free-theory time evolution due Eq. (
383
)
follows by
using the Dirac brackets in Eq. (
396
):

\dot{f}=\kappa^{0}\hskip 1.00006pt\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\phi_{0}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}_{14}
.
For
\kappa^{0}=1/M
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\hat{p}^{\mu}\,,\quad\dot{y}^{\mu}\,=\,0\,,\quad\dot{\lambda}_{\alpha}{}^{I}\,=\,\frac{1}{2iI}\>\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{J}\>W^{a}\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma_{a})_{I}{}^{J}\,.

(399)

In this case, the isotropic rotational inertia
I

is best identified in the internal frame:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2i}\>\Omega^{a}\,(\sigma_{a})_{I}{}^{J}\,:=\,(\hat{\lambda}^{-1})_{I}{}^{\alpha}\,\dot{\hat{\lambda}}_{\alpha}{}^{J}{\quad\implies\quad}W^{a}\,=\,I\hskip 1.00006pt\Omega^{a}\,.

(400)

In fact,
Eq. (
399
) can be shown to be equivalent to Eq. (
394
)
via the map described in Eq. (
71
).
Note also that
\frac{1}{2i}\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma_{a})_{I}{}^{J}

are the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
generators
in the anti-Hermitian convention,
which should explain the
1/2i
factors above:

[\hskip 0.50003pt{\sigma_{a}/2i},{\sigma_{b}/2i}\hskip 0.50003pt]=\varepsilon^{c}{}_{ab}\hskip 1.00006pt(\sigma_{c}/2i)
.

Appendix C
Review of Spinning-Particle Equations of Motion

C.1
BMT Equations

The original statement of
the BMT equations reads
Bargmann:1959gz

\displaystyle\begin{split}\ddot{x}^{\mu}\,&=\,\frac{q}{m}\,F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\,,\quad\dot{\hat{y}}^{\mu}\,=\,\frac{q}{m}\,\bigg[\,{\frac{g}{2}\>F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)-\bigg(\,{1-\frac{g}{2}}\,\bigg)\,\dot{x}^{\mu}\dot{x}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptF^{\kappa}{}_{\nu}(x)}\,\bigg]\,\hat{y}^{\nu}\,,\end{split}

(401)

where a proper time parametrization is assumed
such that
\dot{x}^{2}=-1
.

In this paper,
we advocated a refinement of Eq. (
401
)
into Eq. (
115
),
which we reproduce below for the reader’s sake:

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{q}{m}\,\bigg[\,{\frac{g}{2}\,{*}\hskip-0.50003ptF^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)+\bigg(\,{1-\frac{g}{2}}\,\bigg)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\,{*}\hskip-0.50003ptF^{\kappa}{}_{\nu}(x)}\,\bigg]\,\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(402a)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{y}}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{q}{m}\,\bigg[\,{F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)-\bigg(\,{1-\frac{g}{2}}\,\bigg)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptF^{\kappa}{}_{\nu}(x)}\,\bigg]\,\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(402b)

\displaystyle\dot{p}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{q}{m}\,F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}+\cdots\,.

(402c)

The ellipses signify that
terms of
\mathcal{O}(q^{1+n})
or
\mathcal{O}(\partial^{n}\hskip-1.00006ptF)

are discarded from the right-hand sides
for
n\geq 1
.

We shall emphasize again that
the unit timelike vector
p^{\mu}\hskip-1.00006pt/m

differs
from the four-velocity
\dot{x}^{\mu}

in this refinement.
This mismatch is crucial for
the existence of a well-defined Hamiltonian system
giving rise to Eq. (
402
)
up to the
\mathcal{O}(q^{2})
and
\mathcal{O}(\hat{y}^{2})
truncations,
as is established in Sec.
5.2
.

To elaborate, a necessary condition for the existence of a proper Hamiltonian formulation is that
the following holds for any choice of classical observables
f
and
g
:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,=\,\{\hskip 0.50003pt{\dot{f}},{g}\hskip 0.50003pt\}+\{\hskip 0.50003pt{f},{\dot{g}}\hskip 0.50003pt\}\,.

(403)

This describes a consistency condition
about the time derivative,
mandated by
(a) the Jacobi identity of the Poisson bracket
and
(b) the fact that time evolution is a Hamiltonian action.
Crucially,
the very identity in
Eq. (
403
) will
fail

if one sets
\dot{x}^{\mu}=p^{\mu}\hskip-1.00006pt/m
in Eq. (
402a
)!

Mathematically, Eq. (
403
) states the existence of a Poisson manifold equipped with a Hamiltonian.
In the context of symplectic realizations,
Eq. (
403
) encodes the principle of
symplecticity
,
which states that the theory is Lagrangian
while ensuring the conservation of classical probability.
See the appendix of Ref.
csg

for a detailed discussion.

We shall end with stating the exact equation
that fulfills the consistency condition in Eq. (
403
),
without any truncations.
The symplectic perturbation in Eq. (
117
)
unpacks to

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\,\,=\,\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{qF_{\mu\nu}(x)+c_{1}q\,{*}F_{\mu\nu,\rho}(x)\,\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\,dx^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\nu}\,+\,c_{1}q\,{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)\,d\hat{y}^{\mu}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\nu}\,,

(404)

where we have assumed that
the background is on-shell:

{*}F_{[\mu\nu,\rho]}(x)=0
.
We do not ignore the derivative term in Eq. (
404
).
The exact EoM arises via Eq. (
109
):

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} \frac{p^{\mu}}{m}&-\frac{q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\varepsilon^{\mu\rho\kappa\lambda}\hat{y}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\lambda}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptF_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\\
&-\frac{c_{1}q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{F^{\mu}{}_{\nu,\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\kappa}\dot{x}^{\kappa}-F^{\mu}{}_{\nu,\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\kappa}+\dot{x}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptF_{\rho\sigma,\kappa}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}\hat{y}^{\kappa}}\,\Big)\\
&-\frac{c_{1}q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\\
&+\frac{c_{1}q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{-F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\hat{y}}^{\rho}+\dot{\hat{y}}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt(F_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}\hat{y}^{\sigma})}\,\Big)\,,\end{aligned}

(405a)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{y}}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} &\frac{q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{F_{\rho\sigma}(x)+c_{1}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}F_{\rho\sigma,\kappa}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\kappa}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\\
&+\frac{c_{1}q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\hat{y}}^{\nu}\\
&+\frac{c_{1}q}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\rho}-F^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\rho}+\dot{x}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt(F_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}\hat{y}^{\sigma})}\,\Big)\,,\end{aligned}

(405b)

\displaystyle\dot{p}_{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,q\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{F_{\mu\nu}(x)+c_{1}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}F_{\mu\nu,\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}+c_{1}q\,{*}F_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{\hat{y}}^{\nu}\,.

(405c)

By iterating Eq. (
405
) and truncating, one reproduces Eq. (
402
).

C.2
MPD Equations

The original statement of the MPD equations reads

Mathisson:1937zz
;
Papapetrou:1951pa
;
Dixon:1970zza

\displaystyle\frac{Dp_{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\kappa\lambda}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\kappa\lambda}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\,,\quad\frac{DS^{\mu\nu}}{d\tau}\,=\,-\Big(\,{\dot{x}^{\mu}p^{\nu}-p^{\mu}\dot{x}^{\nu}}\,\Big)\,.

(406a)

The conventional practice
determines
the EoM for
x^{\mu}
by imposing a SSC.
For the covariant SSC
(referred to as Tulcjzew-Dixon SSC
tulczyjew1959motion
;
dixon1964covariant
in this context),

S^{\mu\nu}p_{\nu}=0
,
one finds

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{2p^{2}}\,S^{\mu\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\kappa\lambda}\hskip 0.50003ptR_{\kappa\lambda\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,.

(406b)

To see this, note that
Eqs. (
406a
) and (
406b
) together implies

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\,S^{\mu\rho}\frac{Dp_{\rho}}{d\tau}\,,\quad\frac{DS^{\mu\nu}}{d\tau}+S^{\mu\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{Dp_{\rho}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{p^{\nu}}{p^{2}}-\frac{p^{\mu}}{p^{2}}\hskip 1.00006ptS^{\nu\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{Dp_{\rho}}{d\tau}\,=\,0\,,

(407)

so
(DS^{\mu\nu}\hskip-1.00006pt/d\tau)\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\nu}+S^{\mu\nu}\hskip 0.50003pt(Dp_{\nu}/d\tau)=0
is assured.

We point out that
Eqs. (
406a
) and (
406b
)
can be rewritten
in terms of the pseudovector parametrization of spin

S^{\mu\nu}=\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\hat{y}_{\rho}p_{\sigma}
,
which explicitly imposes the covariant SSC:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\dot{x}^{\mu}\,&=\,\begin{aligned} &\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}-\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}+R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}^{\nu}\hat{p}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{2}+\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{\kappa}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{p}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,\kern-5.0pt\end{aligned}\end{split}

(408a)

\displaystyle\kern-1.99997pt\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,\hat{p}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\lambda}\hat{y}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}+\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{p}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\lambda}\hat{p}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}\,,

(408b)

\displaystyle\kern-1.99997pt\frac{Dp_{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,p_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\lambda\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\lambda}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\,.

(408c)

Here, we have made uses

{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\mu\rho\nu}=0

and
R^{\rho}{}_{\mu\rho\nu}=0
.
By iterating Eq. (
408
) and truncating, one reproduces
Eq. (
159
) stated in the main text.

C.3
QMPD Equations

The quadrupolar extension of the MPD equations
has been envisioned in the literature
since
Refs.
khriplovich1989particle
;
Khriplovich:1997ni
;
Yee:1993ya
,
where
the quadrupolar coupling coefficient
C_{2}

is named the gravimagnetic ratio
\kappa
.
More recent works include
Refs.
Steinhoff:2009tk
;
Harte:2011ku
;
Vines:2016unv
;
Compere:2023alp
;
Ramond:2026fpi
.

In this paper,
we presented a transparent derivation of the QMPD equations
in terms of the covariant symplectic perturbation
\omega^{\prime}
in
Eq. (
161
).
When expanded out, it is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\>p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{{*\hskip-0.50003pt}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\>R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma;\kappa}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\kappa}}\,\bigg)\,\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\rho}{\hskip 1.00006pt{\wedge}\,}dx^{\sigma}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\>p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 0.50003ptD\hat{y}^{\rho}\wedge dx^{\sigma}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}-\frac{C_{2}}{4}\>D\hskip-0.50003ptS_{\kappa\lambda}\wedge{*}R^{\kappa\lambda}{}_{\rho\sigma}\>\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\sigma}}\,,

(409)

where we have intentionally used both
D\hat{y}
and
DS
.
The first line in Eq. (
C.3
)
corresponds to Eq. (
404
)
in light of
the
abelian
double copy mapping
identified in Eq. (
160
).
However, as we experienced in Sec.
7
when deriving the fictitious terms,
the abelian double copy mapping
misses the fact that
S
,
as the (local Lorentz) gravitational charge,
is a variable.
Hence the second line in Eq. (
C.3
) arises.

In fact, the covariant symplectic perturbation for
the
nonabelian
BMT equations in gauge theory backgrounds
(the spinning generalization of the Wong’s equations
Wong:1970fu
)
will precisely
parallel the covariant symplectic perturbation for
the QMPD equations in Eq. (
C.3
),
via the nonabelian double copy mapping

q_{a}F^{a}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)\leftrightarrow-\frac{1}{2}\,S_{\kappa\lambda}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa\lambda}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)

explicated in Ref.
csg

(see also Appendix A of Ref.
ambikerr1
).

By using Eq. (
C.3
) and the universal covariant Poisson bracket relation in Eq. (
151
),
we find the exact and complete version of the QMPD equations:

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} \frac{p^{\mu}}{m}&+\frac{1}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\kappa}\dot{x}^{\kappa}\\
&-\frac{1}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}_{\kappa}\dot{x}^{\kappa}+\dot{x}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\kappa\lambda}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda}}\,\Big)\\
&+\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\varepsilon^{\mu\rho\kappa\lambda}\hat{y}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\lambda}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\rho\sigma;\tau}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\\
&+\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}\\
&+\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{-{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hat{y}^{\rho}}{d\tau}+\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt({*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}\hat{y}^{\sigma})}\,\Big)\end{aligned}

(410a)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}+\frac{C_{2}}{2}\>\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}-\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{*}R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\kappa\lambda}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{2}+\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\kappa\lambda}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt(\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda})}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\rho\sigma,\kappa}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\kappa}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\sigma}\\
&-\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}+p^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hat{y}^{\nu}}{d\tau}\\
&+\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\rho}-{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}^{\mu}{}_{\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\rho}\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\rho}+\dot{x}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt({*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\rho}\hat{y}^{\sigma})}\,\Big)\kern-30.00005pt\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\>\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}+\frac{C_{2}}{2m^{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{\hat{y}^{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptp_{\zeta}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi\kappa\lambda}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\xi}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt(\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda})}\,,\end{aligned}

(410b)

\displaystyle\frac{Dp_{\mu}}{d\tau}\,

\displaystyle=\,\begin{aligned} &p_{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{{*}R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\mu\nu,\rho}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\bigg)\hskip 1.00006pt\dot{x}^{\nu}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,R^{\zeta}{}_{\xi}{}_{\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D\hat{y}^{\nu}}{d\tau}\\
&{\color[rgb]{0,0.5352,0.7305}{}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\>p_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt{*}R^{\kappa}{}_{\zeta\mu\nu}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptR^{\nu}{}_{\xi\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\zeta}\hat{y}^{\xi}\,\hat{y}^{\rho}\dot{x}^{\sigma}+\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,R_{\mu\nu\kappa\lambda}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{D}{d\tau}\hskip 1.00006pt(\hat{y}^{\kappa}p^{\lambda})}\,.\end{aligned}

(410c)

Here, we have made uses

{*}R^{\rho}{}_{\mu\rho\nu}=0

and
R^{\rho}{}_{\mu\rho\nu}=0
.
By iterating Eq. (
410
) and truncating,
one reproduces Eq. (
164
) stated in the main text.

For the reader’s sake, we reproduce Eq. (
164
) below:

\displaystyle\dot{x}^{\mu}\,=\,\frac{p^{\mu}}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\hskip 1.00006pt\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,\Big(\,{R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)+\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{1-C_{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{R^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\frac{D\hat{y}^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,=\,-\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg[\,{\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)-\big(\hskip 1.00006pt{1-C_{2}}\hskip 1.00006pt\big)\,\hat{\delta}^{\mu}{}_{\kappa}\,\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\kappa}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)}\,\bigg]\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

\displaystyle\frac{Dp^{\mu}}{d\tau}\,=\,-\frac{1}{m}\hskip 1.00006pt\Big(\,{{*}\hskip-0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\rho}p^{\sigma}}\,\Big)\hskip 1.00006ptp^{\nu}+\cdots\,,

(411)

where the ellipses signify that terms of
\mathcal{O}(R^{1+n})
,
\mathcal{O}(\hat{y}^{2+n})
, or
\mathcal{O}(D^{n}\hskip-1.00006ptR)
are discarded from the right-hand sides
for
n\geq 1
.

It remains to clarify whether our QMPD equations
agree with the equations stated in the literature

Steinhoff:2009tk
;
Harte:2011ku
;
Vines:2016unv
;
Compere:2023alp
;
Ramond:2026fpi
.
This is a nontrivial process,
as our symplectic perturbation framework
fixes the mass-shell constraint
whereas typical literature
exhibits inclinations toward the so-called “dynamical mass” approach.
We have checked that
our QMPD equations are equivalent to those in Ref.
Compere:2023alp

via
a redefinition of momentum,
a redefinition of mass,
and
a redefinition of the worldline parameter
\tau
.

To elaborate,
recall from Sec.
8
that
the symplectic potential of
a massive spinning particle model
can be represented in the form

p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+(\text{spin term})+(\text{gauge terms})

when minimally coupled,
where the content number of gauge terms
vary over models.
When the quadrupolar coupling coefficient
C_{2}

is introduced via Eq. (
161
),
it becomes

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,p_{\mu}\hskip 0.50003ptR^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hskip 0.50003pt\hat{y}^{\rho}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\sigma}+\cdots\,.

(412a)

Therefore,
without
altering the spin and gauge terms,
one may rewrite Eq. (
412a
) as

\displaystyle\theta\,=\,P_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006ptdx^{\mu}+\cdots\qquad\resizebox{}{}{\scalebox{0.95}[0.95]{{\hbox{{\text{where}}}}}}\qquad P_{\sigma}\,=\,p_{\mu}\hskip 1.00006pt\bigg(\,{\delta^{\mu}{}_{\sigma}-\frac{C_{2}}{2}\,R^{\mu}{}_{\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006pt\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}}\,\bigg)\,.

(412b)

While
Eq. (
412b
) returns the symplectic potential
to its basic (minimally coupled) form,
the mass-shell constraint gets deformed as
(Hamiltonian perturbation)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\phi_{0}\,&=\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{p^{2}+m^{2}}\,\Big)\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\,{P^{2}+m^{2}+C_{2}\,R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptP^{\mu}\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}P^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}(R^{2}\hat{y}^{4})}\,\Big)\,.\end{split}

(413)

This reproduces Ref.
Compere:2023alp
’s dynamical mass function,

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{2}\,=\,m^{2}+C_{2}\,R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(x)\hskip 1.00006ptP^{\mu}\hat{y}^{\nu}\hat{y}^{\rho}P^{\sigma}+\mathcal{O}(S^{3})\,.

(414)

Evidently, this manipulation
counts heavily on
the fact that the quadrupolar coupling in Eq. (
412a
) is a simple
dx
-type term.
It seems unlikely that the dynamical mass function
(Hamiltonian perturbation)
approach will succeed in achieving the exact
root-Kerr
gmoov
;
Kim:2024grz
;
njmagic.1
, Kerr
njmagic.1
, and Kerr-Newman
njmagic.11

black hole couplings
realized in the symplectic perturbation framework,
at least in simple ways
since they crucially involve spin-derivative terms
as explained in Sec.
9.5
.

Appendix D
Definition of Black Hole Coupling

In this appendix, we aim to review the developments
around the question,

.
“
What defines black holes

among all massive

spinning objects
in the point-particle effective theory?
”

We will only cover the “infrared” approaches within the point-particle effective theory,
although a large volume of the current literature
pursues
the determination of black hole coupling
from the ultraviolet (i.e., matching with general relativity).

.
1.
In
linearized gravity
,
the Kerr black hole is defined by
the
unity of multipole coefficients

(i.e.,
three-point spin exponentiation
).

This result is due to the classic works

Hansen:1974zz
;
Geroch:1970cd
;
Newman:1965tw-janis
;
janis1965structure
;
Hernandez:1967zza
;
Thorne:1980ru

in the ’60s and ’70s:
the Kerr solution
is shown to exhibit

C_{\ell}{\>=\>}1
for all
\ell{\>=\>}1,2,3,\cdots
.

According to the traditional definition,
this seems like a grossly non-minimal coupling.
However, a modern analysis

ahh2017
;
Guevara:2018wpp
;
Guevara:2019fsj
;
chkl2019
;
aho2020

shows that it is the simplest coupling
in terms of
a large simplification of
the three-point graviton scattering amplitudes
(cf. Ref.
Holstein:2006wi
).
This simplicity is represented as
a complexification of the impact parameter
that adds spin length as an imaginary component
and is dubbed the
three-point spin exponentiation
.

To elaborate,
these three-point amplitudes
describe the process of the black hole (as a point-particle source) sourcing a single massless quanta.
Thus,
they are synonymous to the linearized part of the Kerr metric

Duff:1973zz
;
Neill:2013wsa
;
Vines:2017hyw
.
In this manner,
it is shown that
the three-point spin exponentiation
is the Newman-Janis shift
in linearized gravity
aho2020
.

The Newman-Janis shift
refers to the property
discovered by Newman and Janis
Newman:1965tw-janis
in 1965
that the Kerr black hole is secretly
a Schwarzschild black hole
displaced into “complex spacetime”
in some rough sense,
in which case the spin length (as ring radius) describes the imaginary direction.
This trick was the very method
that enabled the historical discovery of the Kerr-Newman solution
Newman:1965my-kerrmetric
.
The aspects of Newman-Janis shift
in linearized gravity
have been well-studied from the modern scattering amplitudes perspective by Refs.
ahh2017
;
Guevara:2018wpp
;
Guevara:2019fsj
;
chkl2019
;
aho2020
,
while
its nonlinear aspect has been recently elucidated by Ref.
nja
.

.
2.
In
self-dual gravity
,
the Kerr black hole is defined by
the
n-point spin exponentiation
.

At the nonlinear orders,
it has been unclear
what principle in the point-particle effective theory
defines spinning black holes.
Meanwhile,
explorations via scattering amplitudes
investigated the
n
-point Compton scattering process
in which the Kerr black hole
receives
(n{\hskip 1.00006pt-\hskip 1.00006pt}2)
gravitons,
where
n\geq 3
.
This research showed that
the
n
-point gravitational Compton amplitudes
can exhibit the spin exponentiation property
as a theoretically allowed possibility,
iff all the helicities of the gravitons are the same

Johansson:2019dnu
;
Aoude:2020onz
;
Lazopoulos:2021mna
.
21
21

21

The mixed-helicity Compton amplitudes are plagued by a contact term issue,
first noticed in Ref.
ahh2017
.

Therefore, by extending the definition of the simplicity of scattering amplitudes,
it has been believed that
this “
n
-point spin exponentiation”
for all
n=3,4,5,6,\cdots

may define the black hole coupling at all nonlinear orders
within the
same-helicity
sector
as a beautiful possibility,
yet without a necessary physical reason.

This scattering amplitudes statement
is equivalent to the proposition that
the point-particle effective action of the Kerr black hole
is unique
(up to worldline field redefintions)
in background self-dual spacetimes.
A self-dual spacetime
describes a formal complexified limit of spacetime
in which the Riemann tensor becomes self-dual:

{*}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}={+i\hskip 0.50003ptR_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}
.
A well-established fact is that
the same-helicity gravitational Compton amplitudes
due to incoming positive-helicity gravitons
describe
scattering processes in
self-dual background spacetimes;
see Figure 1 in Ref.
Mason:2009afn

and also

So far, the construction of the exact worldline effective theory of the Kerr black hole
may have been
based on model-specific perspectives.
An early sketch was given in Ref.
gmoov

by extending
the Levi-Steinhoff action
in the spherical top model,
which identified the Newman-Janis shift
as an important clue.
This work provided valuable intuitions and insights,
although
the subtleties of spin gauge redundancy,
geodesic deviation, and integrability of complex structure
are not
clearly resolved
and
the explicit expressions are limited to the linear-in-Riemann order.
In the meantime, Ref.
ambikerr1
provided an approach based on
the Kähler geometry of massive twistor space,
unearthing a unique geometrical perspective on the Newman-Janis shift
that was also demonstrated in Ref.
sst-asym
.
A recent work
bonocore2025higher

seemed to ponder on the relevance of
supersymmetry as a model-specific feature of a fermionic model.

Eventually,

;

While
Refs.
njmagic.1
;
njmagic.11

paved a top-down pathway
to the problem
from massive twistor theory,
Ref.
probe-nj
provided a complementary, bottom-up view
that emphasizes model independence.
Ref.
probe-nj

defines
an EoM-level implementation of
the Newman-Janis algorithm
in terms of the universal variables
(x,\hat{y},p)

of the spinning probes.
A corollary is
the aforementioned hidden symmetry in the self-dual sector.
Notably,
the conserved charges of the spinning probe
are directly obtained
by dynamically Newman-Janis shifting
the conserved charges of the scalar probe.

In this universal manner,
Ref.
probe-nj
also suggests
a natural proposal for uplifting
the self-dual sector couplings
to the full nonlinear sector
in a rather model-independent fashion.
In this case, the principle that defines black holes
is “orbit-spin duality,”
a generalization of an old idea due to Newman and Winicour

in curved backgrounds
